## Decisions Added 2026-06-19

- **Phase 2.6 scoring-config input convention:** Optimizer and trade-value service entry points accept the whole league scoring-config row directly, through `opts.scoringConfig`, or as a separate trailing argument. Existing option objects and all current call sites remain valid; explicit legacy options take precedence over config values.
- **Phase 2.6 optimizer config paths:** `default_scoring_rules.optimizer.lineup_min_delta`, `waiver_min_delta`, and `waiver_limit` parameterize lineup and waiver thresholds. Missing or malformed values preserve the existing `0.5`, `0`, and `5` defaults.
- **Phase 2.6 trade config paths:** Top-level `scoring_format`, `league_scarcity_weights[].baseline_points`, and `league_scarcity_weights[].scarcity_weight` parameterize replacement/scarcity math. `default_scoring_rules.trade_value.neutral_band` and `scarcity_signal_weight` parameterize verdict behavior. Invalid config-only weights fall back safely; explicit options win.
- **No config loader in Phase 2.6:** This is a pure-service seam only. No route, Supabase query, migration, RLS, service-role, or production behavior changed because the Phase 1.4 scoring schema remains review-only.
- **Phase 2.6 merged and deployed:** PR #49 was squash-merged to `main` as `93e1a7176e26c715d34b5337df66182a6a828eb0`. `Deploy to Hostinger KVM1` run `27834697621` passed quality, backend tests, dependency audit, both frontend builds, API/cron image publishing, KVM1 restart, and workflow health smoke. Independent production checks returned health `ok`, ready `ready`, and homepage `200`. Rollback is a PR reverting `93e1a71`, which rebuilds and redeploys the prior source through the normal main workflow.
- **Phase 2.7 Demo Mode contract:** Public `GET /api/demo` returns `corvus-demo.v1`: a deterministic normalized sample roster plus an optimizer-backed Omen envelope. Demo is a first-class mode (`mode: "demo"`, `is_demo: true`, `is_live: false`, `is_mock: false`), not a live fallback and not the development mock contract.
- **Demo isolation:** Demo Mode reads no auth, user, Supabase, provider, or LLM state. It never auto-merges into a connected league; leaving demo for live data requires an explicit frontend action. Fixture interactions are marked ineligible for analytics and LLM training.
- **Demo frontend interpretation:** `mode` is authoritative. Frontend Phase 2.7 must add a persistent Demo Mode label and a `demo` signal treatment; it must not infer that `is_mock: false` means live.
- **Phase 2.7 merged and deployed:** PR #51 was squash-merged to `main` as `cccc857f69364146125ebdd87caab7bb843f234a`. `Deploy to Hostinger KVM1` run `27842680052` passed clean-runner tests, production audit, both frontend builds, API/cron image publishing, KVM1 restart, and workflow health smoke. Independent production canary passed 24/24 root/`www` checks with p95 242 ms, including the public Demo contract, health/readiness, homepage, HTTPS redirects, and HSTS. Rollback is a PR reverting `cccc857` so the normal `main` workflow rebuilds and redeploys the prior source.
- **Phase 2.8 Sleeper live draft tracking contract:** Three new `requireAuth` routes — `GET /api/sleeper/draft?leagueId=`, `GET /api/sleeper/draft/:draftId`, `GET /api/sleeper/draft/:draftId/state?since=` — exposing `sleeper-draft-list.v1`, `sleeper-draft-meta.v1`, and `sleeper-draft-state.v1` envelopes. Subscription not required (Draft Assistant is free; this surface aligns with that posture). No new dependencies; reuses the adapter's existing `axios` + `@upstash/redis` plumbing.
- **Phase 2.8 Lazy Sync cursor choice:** Frontend polls with a numeric `since` cursor on `pick_no` (not ETag/If-None-Match). Sleeper picks are append-only with monotonically increasing `pick_no`, so a cursor avoids hashing/304 semantics. Server returns `200` with `picks_since: []` + `has_new_picks: false` when the caller is current — no `304` body.
- **Phase 2.8 poll pacing:** Status-aware `poll_after_seconds` (`pre_draft: 60`, `drafting: 8`, `paused: 30`, `complete: 300`, `unknown: 30`) plus advisory `debounce_ms: 5000`. Frontend polls at `max(poll_after_seconds * 1000, debounce_ms)`. Server enforces upstream protection via the existing `generalRateLimit` and a Redis micro-cache (`picks` 5s, `meta` 60s) in `src/adapters/sleeper.js`. No per-user in-memory debounce middleware was added — added a follow-up note instead to revisit once real load lands.
- **Phase 2.8 draft-type coverage:** Snake and linear/standard drafts compute `on_the_clock` server-side from `total_picks_taken`. Auction drafts pass through with `on_the_clock: null` and the raw Sleeper `type` so the frontend can detect and render differently. No IDP/DEF enrichment beyond what Sleeper returns in pick `metadata`.
- **Phase 2.8 verification:** `npm test` 341/341 (was 312; +29 across `test/sleeperDraftService.test.js`, `test/sleeperDraftRoute.test.js`, `test/sleeperDraftAdapter.test.js`). `npm audit --audit-level=moderate` 0 vulnerabilities. Contract block + frontend polling snippet at `Blueprints/handoffs/backend-to-frontend.md`; dated handoff at `Blueprints/handoffs/2026-06-19-phase2-8-sleeper-draft-handoff.md`. No SQL, secret, package, env, Supabase, or deploy change.
- **Phase 2.8 merged and deployed:** Implementation commit `8563cc4`; PR #53 squash-merged to `main` 2026-06-19T20:50:05Z as `8c6c3bd183ac0f483d12aecf9a6c71cdf4efafe1`. `Deploy to Hostinger KVM1` (`.github/workflows/deploy.yml`) primary self-hosted-runner path completed and the KVM1 container restarted (`/api/health` uptime confirmed at 282s in the 21:01 UTC smoke window, well under the pre-merge `7642s`/`7893s` measurements). Independent production smoke 2026-06-19 21:01 UTC: `/api/health` `ok`; `/api/ready` `ready` with Supabase reachable and Stripe/Yahoo/Redis/LLM/OpenWeather optional services all `true`; `www.slopssaloon.com/api/health` `ok`; `/api/version` `200`; `/api/sleeper/draft`, `/api/sleeper/draft/:draftId`, and `/api/sleeper/draft/:draftId/state` all return `401 Missing bearer token` (no auth) and `401 Invalid or expired token` (bad bearer) — confirms `requireAuth` is gating before query/path validation. Rollback target: revert `8c6c3bd` through a PR; the normal `main` workflow rebuilds and redeploys the prior API and cron images.
- **Phase 2.8 post-deploy audit hardening:** The audit found and fixed three P1s before the next merge: active polling was 8 seconds instead of Justin's approved high-frequency posture; route auth did not verify the requested league/draft belonged to the user's active connection; and responses exposed other managers' raw Sleeper user IDs. The corrected contract is two-second active / 30-second low, connected-league-only, and caller-relative (`user_draft_slot`, `is_user_pick`). A 900-call/minute process budget and safe error envelopes were added. Focused 40/40; full 352/352; audit 0; review has no remaining P0/P1.
- **Phase 2.8 hardening merged and deployed:** PR #56 was squash-merged to `main` as `b1ec05d2ba0042aab93dc6ccff6d71d86ca8312a`. `Deploy to Hostinger KVM1` run `27853988393` passed quality (tests, audit, primary and legacy builds), API/cron image builds, KVM1 restart, workflow health smoke, and log tail; both containers started and the API reported healthy. Independent 2026-06-20 00:08 UTC smoke returned `200` health, ready, version, and `www` health; all three draft routes returned `401 Missing bearer token` without auth. Authenticated connected-league behavior was not production-probed because no user bearer token was used. Rollback target: revert `b1ec05d` through a PR so the normal `main` workflow rebuilds and redeploys the prior images.
- **Sleeper platform-risk acceptance:** The public API docs describe a free read-only tokenless API, while the June 1, 2026 General Terms restrict business/third-party-benefit use. Justin explicitly directed free Corvus to ship high-frequency mode and revert future traffic if Sleeper objects. This is founder risk acceptance, not legal clearance; low mode does not cure the ambiguity and rollback cannot erase earlier requests.

## Decisions Added 2026-06-20

- **Phase 1.5e — 32-team visual QA pass, secondary-scheme verdict:** All nine secondary-scheme teams (PIT, HOU, LV, NYG, WAS, CHI, GB, LAR, SEA) **stay on secondary**. Production browser sweep against `slopssaloon.com/account/appearance` confirmed each curated accent carries team identity better than the primary would in every case (Steelers gold on slate, Packers gold on dark green, Bears orange on navy, etc. are all on-brand and iconic). Justin can still override per-team, but no flip is recommended on visual evidence. Full audit doc: `Blueprints/audits/2026-06-20-phase1-5e-32-team-visual-qa.md`.
- **"Team mode = always dark" rule flagged for removal — Phase 1.5f scoped.** Justin called the rule into question mid-sweep ("maybe teams shouldnt always be in dark mode. it should be in a per use basis"). Source inspection on `frontend/src/lib/teamTemplate.js` confirmed `SURFACE_RECIPES` hardcodes L=8–10 (dark-only) and the page-system spec (Phase 1.3, line 103) actually anticipated *both* light and dark contrast sweeps — the "Always dark" implementation was a Phase 1.5 PR1 simplification, not the spec's intent. Removing it requires a code spike (Phase 1.5f): extend recipes to a light/dark 2-axis map (L≈92–95 for light); make `getTeamTemplate()` theme-aware; decide Bred light-mode fallback (probably Template 1 in light — pure-white "Bred" stops being Bred); add or split `textSafe` for light-canvas accents (lower L instead of raising it); update the Mode picker copy from "Always dark" to "Tracks your light/dark preference"; re-run the 32-team WCAG sweep against light bg; bundle the long-standing `--color-text-on-accent` token fix from Phase 1.5b. Naive-flip evidence (`ss_0264qnlsz`) shows the page breaks without these changes. Rough complexity: medium. Awaiting Justin's go/no-go on prioritization.
- **Three new defects filed against the team-theming system (no fixes shipped this pass):** **1.5e-defect-1 (P1)** — LV Raiders accent `textSafe` lift turns near-neutral silver (`#A5ACAF`) into washed cool-blue (`#8ab7ca`); CTA reads as muted lavender, not silver. Fix is to clamp hue-shift for low-saturation accents (S<10%). **1.5e-defect-2 (P2)** — Bred template 6 applies `textSafe` to `#A71930` which collapses Falcons varsity-red to the same coral as HOU and NYG; Bred should arguably bypass `textSafe` to preserve identity. **1.5e-defect-3 (P3 note)** — HOU/NYG/ATL currently share the identical lifted accent `#e3455e` at runtime; low-priority unless defects 1 and 2 don't differentiate the palettes enough. All three are documented in the audit doc with recommended fixes.

- **Phase 1.5e scope correction — full-32 identity audit shipped (2026-06-20, same day).** Justin clarified the original "both light and dark" ask was about *per-team fan-perceived identity*, not user toggles: "i wan to use all of the teams colors and then we can add colors we know will make the page better but we need to use their colors. for teams like atlanta, where they there is popular jordan show that has a similar color scheme we use that. for example arizona cold use 'Toro Bravo' as insparation." Original audit was too narrow (9 secondary + 2 specials) and answered the wrong policy question. **Superseding audit:** `Blueprints/audits/2026-06-20-phase1-5e-32-team-identity-audit.md` covers all 32 teams with fan-identity sentences, cultural anchors (Jordan/AM97/Vice/Poe/Mardi Gras/OutKast/etc.), per-team axis decisions, and re-scoped Phase 1.5f. First-pass audit marked superseded in place; kept on disk as scope-correction audit-trail.
- **Six teams flagged for LIGHT Team-mode axis** (fan identity is inherently light, not dark): **MIA** Dolphins (Miami Vice + South Beach pastel), **IND** Colts (iconic white helmet horseshoe), **LAC** Chargers (1960s beach), **DAL** Cowboys (silver/white Lone Star), **CAR** Panthers (Carolina blue + UNC heritage — flagged for Justin's call, predator-vs-tobacco-road tradeoff), **ARI** Cardinals (Jordan 6 "Toro Bravo" red-on-white). Other 26 stay dark. **This reframes Team mode from "always dark" to "per-team light/dark axis"** — the surface axis is an entity fact, not a user preference. User-axis toggling remains an open separate question (probably handled by System / Corvus modes; Team mode honors the team).
- **NYG flipped to royal-blue primary accent (contra first-pass verdict).** Resolves the HOU/NYG/ATL hue-collision (defect 1.5e-defect-3) by removing NYG from the shared coral group, AND aligns with the "Big Blue" fan identity. First-pass said "all 9 secondary stay on secondary"; full-scope audit revises to "8 of 9 stay on secondary, NYG flips to primary." Other 8 secondary-scheme teams confirmed staying on secondary with cultural-anchor justifications (Steelers gold = US Steel logo, Packers gold = Lambeau tundra, etc.).
- **Four new defects filed in the full-scope audit:** **1.5e-defect-4 (P2)** PHI accent currently renders too cyan (close to JAX) — shift to true midnight green `#004C54`. **1.5e-defect-5 (P2)** TB surface should be pewter `#34302B` (fan identity = pirate pewter helmet), not the current bloody-red — re-derive surface from secondary. **1.5e-defect-6 (P2)** SF accent textSafe-lifts to coral — deepen to true `#AA0000`. **1.5e-defect-7 (P1, escalated from Phase 1.5b)** `--color-text-on-accent` token still missing in `index.css` — required for every light-accent team (SEA, gold accents) and for every team flipping to light axis in Phase 1.5f.
- **Phase 1.5f spike re-scoped** from "add light recipes so user can flip" to a richer 11-point goal set: per-team `surfaceAxis` field in `nflTeams.js`; two-axis `SURFACE_RECIPES`; theme-aware `getTeamTemplate()`; sat-aware `textSafe()`; Bred bypass; per-team accent overrides for HOU/NYG/PHI/SF; TB surface re-derivation; `--color-text-on-accent` token; two-axis WCAG re-sweep; Mode picker copy update; `page-system.md` spec update. Complexity medium-large.
- **New methodology doc landed at `Brand/entity-identity-theming.md`.** Per Justin's directive ("we need to add this type of clarification to our template so that when we make the next app we have a head start at desining"). Product-agnostic methodology for any future SLOPS product where users self-identify with an entity (sports team, music act, anime franchise, brand). Six core principles (identity is found not derived; per-entity light/dark axis; cultural anchors with citations; textSafe trap; special-case templates; hue-collision check), 10-step audit process, 6 anti-patterns, adoption guide, promotion-to-L0/L1 trigger (second product adoption). Currently L2 — flagged for promotion when a second SLOPS product needs entity-affinity theming.
- **Three Justin calls answered 2026-06-20 (folded into the audit):** **(1)** CAR Panthers axis = **LIGHT** (Carolina blue + UNC heritage wins over the nocturnal-predator read). **(2)** Per-team motif flourishes are **IN** the roadmap — promoted to a new **Phase 1.5g** (motif hairlines, typography flourishes, animated cultural moments — CIN tiger-stripe, Saints Mardi-purple, NE minuteman, Bears '85 bold-italic, NO Mardi Gras week, BUF playoff-snow, etc.); requires a richer template grammar spec at 1.5g kickoff. **(3)** Cultural-anchor citation in product UI = **YES** — added as Phase 1.5f scope item #12 (new `culturalAnchor` field in `nflTeams.js` with `{name, year?, kind}` shape; surfaced on `/account/appearance` tile detail; does not enter the rest of the app shell). Methodology doc updated to recommend this pattern for any future adopter. Still-open: WAS Mardi-style alternate surface (not asked about today — can fold into the 1.5g grammar conversation).

## Decisions Added 2026-06-21

- **Corvus is the company-baseline proving ground for SLOPS skills and procedures.** Every current skill now has an explicit Corvus route in `Blueprints/playbooks/skill-activation-runbook.md`: required core gate, conditional trigger, event/milestone trigger, parked gate, or retired prohibition. Every task leaves a skill receipt in its handoff and `Blueprints/playbooks/skill-usage-ledger.md`; skills are not forced when irrelevant, but the N/A reason is explicit. The build loop and Done files now link the company-baseline playbook.
- **Learning/community sequencing:** `slops-learning-loop` is parked until Corvus passes Release Done plus seven stable days; its first cycle teaches Justin why the Corvus stack exists and produces before-season-end improvements. `slops-community-needs-research` is outside Corvus and parked until Justin explicitly opens a far-future community-product discovery phase.

- **Phase 1.5h shipped — multi-color official palettes + special cultural variants (doctrine reversal of 1.5f single-accent).** Justin doctrine 2026-06-21 (two parts): (1) **No team page should be in stylistic dark mode.** The page can appear dark because the team's canonical world color is dark (PIT black, BAL purple, ATL Stankonia, LV black), but Corvus never imposes a dark-mode-on-team-accent style. The team's palette IS the surface. (2) **Every team must surface its full official palette on every themed page.** The Phase 1.5/1.5f single-accent model failed both rules — 32-team audit showed every team rendered ONE color (with 8 secondary-swap teams showing 2), and white was missing from every team. Phase 1.5h replaces with: per-team `palettes` array (Official + optional Special), explicit `surfaceRole` per palette, role-keyed CSS tokens (`--color-team-primary/secondary/tertiary/neutral/mute/pop`), derived accent that falls through to secondary when surface == primary (fixes green-on-green and black-on-black CTA visibility), variant toggle on `/account/appearance`, and cultural-anchor citation extended from `/account/appearance` to all themed pages via `Footer`.
- **30 of 32 teams have a "Special" cultural-variant palette in 1.5h.** Names are tied to the team's region/music/food/history — explicitly NO Nike/Jordan trademarks (sneaker colorways like "Bred" / "Vice" / "Toro Bravo" / "Pine Green" / "Tiffany" / "Concord" / "Bordeaux" / "Grape" / "Silver Bullet" retired). New names: ATL Stankonia (OutKast 2000), MIA Calle Ocho (Little Havana), MIN Paisley Park (Prince), DET 8 Mile (Eminem / Motor City), BAL Poe's Raven (Edgar Allan Poe), LV Black Hole (Raiders fan section), NYJ Hempstead Green (Long Island), JAX Marsh Teal (St. Johns River), WAS Go-Go Burgundy (DC music tradition), ARI Sonoran Sunset (Sonoran Desert). Plus the pre-approved cultural anchors: NO Mardi Gras, KC Arrowhead BBQ, SEA Pike Place Grunge, CHI '85 Bears, GB Lambeau Tundra, SF Gold Rush, PIT US Steel. Plus bonus specials Justin approved: BUF Wing Sauce, NE Bunker Hill, CIN Skyline Chili, HOU Mission Control, IND Loud House, TEN Music Row, DEN Mile High Sunset, LAC Pacific Beach, DAL Tom Landry, NYG Empire State, PHI Liberty Bell, CAR Tobacco Road, TB Gasparilla. **CLE and LAR are official-only** — no natural cultural special found without forcing it.
- **Surface rule for 32 teams (Justin Q1 answer 2026-06-21):** Per-team palette role assignment — `surfaceRole: 'neutral'` for ~10 teams whose merchandise reads "color on white" (KC, MIA, IND, LAC, DAL, ARI, CAR, plus Cowboys-style), `surfaceRole: 'primary'` for ~18 teams whose identity IS the dominant brand color (BUF royal, GB green, PIT black, BAL purple, NE navy, DEN orange, LV black, NYG royal, PHI midnight green, WAS burgundy, CHI navy, DET Honolulu blue, MIN purple, LAR royal, SF red, SEA navy, CLE brown, JAX teal, TEN navy, HOU steel blue, NYJ pine green), `surfaceRole: 'mute'` for the 4 teams whose canonical world is near-black despite a vivid primary (ATL Bred, NO Saints black, TB pewter, CIN tiger black). KC follows the explicit (c) call from Justin: white world with red CTAs and gold highlights.
- **Phase 1.5f doctrine retired in place.** The `surfaceAxis: 'light' | 'dark'` per-team field, two-axis `SURFACE_RECIPES`, `textSafe()` saturation-clamp algorithm, `accentLifted` per-team overrides, and `surfaceFrom: 'secondary'` heuristic are all gone. Doctrine moved from "stylistic axis assigned per team" to "axis is a consequence of which palette role serves as the surface." Phase 1.5e and 1.5f audit docs remain on disk as audit-trail; their `surfaceAxis` recommendations no longer drive code.
- **Phase 1.5f shipped — theme-aware team palettes + cultural-anchor citations (single-PR spike).** Twelve sub-items landed in one PR per Justin's "one pr" directive on 2026-06-21: per-team `surfaceAxis` field in `nflTeams.js` (6 light: MIA/IND/LAC/DAL/CAR/ARI; other 26 dark), two-axis `SURFACE_RECIPES` in `teamTemplate.js`, theme-aware `getTeamTemplate()` returning `axis` + `accentBg` + `textOnAccent`, saturation-aware `textSafe()` with hue-clamp for low-saturation accents (fixes LV silver — defect 1.5e-defect-1), Bred template-6 bypass of `textSafe` (preserves Falcons varsity-red — defect 1.5e-defect-2), per-team `accentLifted` overrides for HOU (Battle Red preserved) / PHI (midnight green at altitude — defect 1.5e-defect-4) / SF (49ers red preserved — defect 1.5e-defect-6), `surfaceFrom` field used by TB to re-derive surface from secondary (pewter not blood — defect 1.5e-defect-5), NYG accent flipped from secondary red to primary royal blue with scheme `standard` (defect 1.5e-defect-3 resolution), `--color-text-on-accent` token added to all 3 root blocks in `index.css` plus per-axis runtime override in `themeMode.js` (defect 1.5e-defect-7 resolution from Phase 1.5b carryover), Mode picker copy updated ("Light or dark to match your team's true colors"), `culturalAnchor: { name, year?, kind }` field added to all 32 teams with `CulturalAnchorAttribution` component surfaced on `/account/appearance` tile detail, `page-system.md` spec updated (new "Team mode is per-entity light/dark, NOT per-user" section + cultural-anchor attribution rules + `/account/appearance` row marked Phase 1.5f shipped). Hardcoded `color: '#0A0A0B'` literals swept to `var(--color-text-on-accent)` in `Onboarding.jsx` (4 sites), `Appearance.jsx` LivePreview CTA, and `OmenFeedback.jsx` (2 sites).
- **`readableOn()` switched from luminance heuristic to actual WCAG contrast computation.** Old algorithm used `relLum > 0.45` to pick foreground polarity; this misjudged mid-luminance teals (MIA aqua), oranges (CIN/DEN), and silvers (LV) where black-text vs white-text contrast doesn't follow luminance intuition. New algorithm computes both options and picks the one with higher contrast ratio. Fixed 8 of the 11 contrast-sweep failures without any other change.
- **Two-axis WCAG sweep doctrine: programmatic + known-marginals doctrine.** New `frontend/scripts/contrast-sweep.mjs` computes 32 teams × 2 surfaces × 4 contrast cells (body/surface, body/card, CTA text/bg, accent/surface) and emits a markdown audit at `Blueprints/audits/2026-06-21-phase1-5f-two-axis-wcag-sweep.md`. Three "failures" are baked into the script as **accepted known marginals** with rationale: **KC** Chiefs red and **DET** Lions blue CTA cells (4.20 and 4.34 vs AA-normal 4.5, both pass AA-large 3.0 which is the appropriate threshold for CTAs in the 16–18 px semibold range; identity-preservation wins over a brand-color edit), and **ATL** Bred accent-on-surface (2.72) which is by design — Bred (template 6) uses the accent as the call-to-action FILL on pure black, not as text painted ON the surface, so the metric is informational only. Re-runs will continue to show 0 unexpected failures unless a new defect appears.
- **`accentLifted` per-team override pattern established.** Three teams (HOU/PHI/SF) carry an explicit `{ dark?: string, light?: string }` override that bypasses `textSafe()`'s algorithmic lift. Used only where the algorithm provably loses brand identity — HOU Battle Red coraling out, PHI midnight green cyaning out, SF 49ers red coraling out. Other 29 teams use the algorithm directly. Future teams should reach for `accentLifted` sparingly — most identity-preserving fixes are better handled by improving the algorithm.

## Decisions Added 2026-06-22

- **Phase 1.5g split — motif grammar spec authored before any code.** Path A from kickoff plan-approval. Phase 1.5g splits into four phases — 1.5g.0 (this spec, doc-only), 1.5g.1 (motif schema + 4-team hairline ship), 1.5g.2 (typeFlourish schema + NE small-caps eyebrow), 1.5g.3 (culturalMoment schema + DET/NO/GB moments). v1 grammar adopts the **category-axis** shape: three independent optional arrays on each team root — `motifs`, `typeFlourishes`, `culturalMoments` — each with its own resolver (`frontend/src/lib/motifs.js`, `lib/typeFlourishes.js`, `lib/culturalMoments.js`), its own CSS-var namespace (`--motif-*`, `--type-flourish-*`, `--moment-*`), and its own AAA gate. Canonical spec at `Blueprints/specs/team-motif-grammar.md`. Page System addendum at `Blueprints/specs/page-system.md` adds a Motif / Moment posture column.
- **Why category-axis won.** Three competing proposals reviewed by three lenses (brand-voice, engineering-tractability, maintenance-cost). Category-axis-grammar won on every tiebreaker — average score 5.67 vs 4.67 for both Maximum-flexibility and Tier-Based; one weak verdict vs two for the others; strongest brand-voice score (7) of the three. Brand-voice is Justin's priority lens. Independent categories let motif, type, and moment ship and revert independently; the Omen card stays presentation-worthy by schema (`excludesOmenCard: true` hard-typed literal); the typography lock is preserved (Alegreya / Alegreya Sans only, no new `@import`); the mock/live rail is enforced via `mockBadge.required: true` hard-typed literal; cultural moments never render on `/omen` or `/trade` (recommendation surfaces stay neutral).
- **Grafts from runners-up applied to the winner.** From Maximum-flexibility: motif `appliesTo` allow-list pattern; reduced-motion encoded as a literal schema value per category. From Tier-Based: `mockBadge.required: true` literal type; boot-time `assertCategoryShape()` validator pattern (replaces self-attested `trademarkSafe: true` with a real gate); sprint split mapping 1:1 to category.
- **Doctrine fixes applied to the winner before adoption.** `trademarkSafe: true` literal demoted to `trademarkReview: 'pending' | 'counsel-approved' | 'self-assessed'` — production renders `self-assessed` AND `counsel-approved`, `pending` suppressed. v1 team count tightened from 9 to 6 (PIT, GB, DET, NO, MIA, NE) to remove all trademark-adjacent shapes from 1.5g.1 — 1.5g.1 ships motifs for 4 teams (PIT, MIA, NO, GB), 1.5g.2 adds NE typeFlourish, 1.5g.3 adds DET / NO / GB cultural moments. All theatrical eyebrow strings rejected (Poe "Nevermore", OutKast "F.I.L.A.", Prince "Paisley Park is in your heart", Pirates "Hoist the colors"); cultural moment eyebrows must pass `slops-ux-copy`. BAL dropCap on Omen, CIN italic on Omen titles rejected (Trust pillar — recommendations read identically across teams). GB Lambeau Tundra eyebrow strips the unverifiable weather claim; remains manual-flag activation with working-draft "Lambeau, manually painted." subject to `slops-ux-copy`. NO Mardi Gras `scope` restricted to `['app','account']` only — no paint over `/omen`, `/ledger`, `/standings`, `/football` recommendation chrome. The "league-event neutrals whitelist" open question is closed — moment eyebrow colors are palette-sourced or the moment is omitted. Season-week activation deferred to 1.5g.4 (no week-clock data source in v1).
- **v1 scope.** 6 teams across three sprints. PIT (motif), GB (motif + moment), DET (moment), NO (motif + moment), MIA (motif), NE (typeFlourish).
- **Risks carried forward (recorded for 1.5g.1+ pickup).** (a) `window.__corvusDataMode` route-level mock/live indicator does not exist today — hard prerequisite for 1.5g.3; built inside that phase if missing. (b) Counsel-approval is a process gate, not an automated check — `self-assessed` is v1 baseline for pure-geometry hairlines; counsel sign-off required before any trademark-adjacent shape renders. (c) Cultural moment activation reads client clock — midnight rollover on long-lived tabs won't activate moments until next theme event (revisit 1.5g.4). (d) Google Fonts CSS2 OpenType feature retention (`"smcp" 1`) is unverified at served-CSS level — Phase 1.5g.2 must spike font-feature against served font before NE merges, or self-host Alegreya Sans. (e) Italic-700 not loaded — `assertCategoryShape()` throws on `style: 'italic'` + `weight: 700`. (f) `color-mix(in srgb, ...) with calc(unitless * 100%)` has historical Safari 16.x quirks — 1.5g.3 done-when requires Safari smoke or pre-resolved-hex fallback. (g) `nflCalendar.js` becomes annual maintenance debt — owner Justin, cadence pre-season August, skill `slops-context-markdown`. (h) Cross-tab localStorage sync for `corvus.theme.moments` flags deferred to 1.5g.4.
- **Deferred from v1 (in spec Open questions).** Animated motifs; per-mode motif color override; cross-tab moment sync; multi-window moments; self-hosted Alegreya; CLE / LAR Specials; WAS Mardi alternate; ATL Bred motif; PIT three-diamond corner; IND horseshoe; KC arrowhead; MIN star/Skol pattern; LAR Hollywood star; BAL Poe italic ligatures; ATL Stankonia motif; TB Gasparilla; season-week activation; Sleeper draft-state-driven moments; audio cues; scroll-linked motion; time-of-day surface shifts; haptic feedback; eyebrow localization; per-user moment opt-out for date-range moments.

- **App rename active pass completed — Corvus → Omen.** Public/product identity is now Omen across active app code, source filenames, route copy, local contracts, package metadata, design docs, and L0/L1/L2 routing wrappers. Compatibility retained deliberately: `/corvus` redirects to `/about`, old `corvus.*` localStorage keys migrate to `omen.*`, and legacy `CORVUS_*` env flags remain fallbacks behind new `OMEN_*` names. At this point no production deploy, database migration, GitHub repo rename, GHCR image rename, Oracle/KVM path change, or container rename had been performed; those were completed in the later operational cutover.

## Decisions Added 2026-06-23

- **Omen operational rename OP2 repo-side seed patch prepared.** The primary KVM1 deploy workflow and Tailscale fallback workflow now publish dual GHCR tags for the same API and cron images: existing Corvus tags remain for rollback while new Omen tags are seeded (`ghcr.io/justinduverge-design/omen:main`, `ghcr.io/justinduverge-design/omen-cron:main`). Cache scopes intentionally stay `corvus-api` and `corvus-cron` during this seed phase to avoid adding build-cache risk. The live deploy path still uses `/opt/corvus`, Compose project `corvus`, and `corvus_api`/`corvus_cron`; no push, workflow run, KVM1 action, repo rename, DNS/SSL, secret, database, or production mutation was performed.
- **Omen operational rename OP3 repo-side compose prep prepared.** Checked-in compose assets now target Omen identity: `deploy/hostinger/docker-compose.prod.yml` uses project `omen`, Omen GHCR images, `omen_api` / `omen_cron`, and `omen_network`; local `docker-compose.yml` mirrors those names; `docker-compose.hostinger.yml` prefers `OMEN_ENV_FILE` with `CORVUS_ENV_FILE` retained as a one-release fallback; checked-in Hostinger Nginx sample moved from `nginx-corvus.conf` to `nginx-omen.conf`. This prepares the files that should be copied to `/opt/omen` later, but does not mutate KVM1. No runner label, `/opt/omen` directory, env file, Nginx production site, container, DNS/SSL, secret, database, or production state was changed.
- **Omen operational rename execution approved and KVM1 runner prepared.** Justin instructed Codex to continue until the rename is done, which is treated as approval to execute the operational rename while preserving rollback artifacts and keeping secrets private. GitHub runner inspection found KVM1 runner `corvus-kvm1-deploy` online with existing `corvus-deploy`; the `omen-deploy` label was added while retaining `corvus-deploy` for rollback. A manual-only guarded workflow, `.github/workflows/omen-operational-cutover.yml`, now defines the `/opt/omen` cutover with explicit confirmation, env-file copy without printing secrets, Omen image pre-pull, Corvus project stop, Omen project start, health/readiness verification, and automatic rollback attempt if Omen fails after Corvus is stopped.
- **Omen cutover first attempt stopped safely at prep.** Workflow run `27995403847` failed before preflight or container changes because KVM1 runner user `justin` can use Docker but cannot create `/opt/omen` via passwordless sudo (`sudo: a password is required`). Production stayed healthy and already reported `service: "omen-api"` from the seeded image on the old Corvus compose project. The cutover workflow now uses a short-lived Docker helper, through the runner's existing Docker access, when direct `/opt` write and passwordless sudo are unavailable; it copies deploy files into `/opt/omen` and preserves the existing env-file permissions with `cp -p` without printing secrets.
- **Omen operational rename completed.** Cutover run `27995488318` installed `/opt/omen/deploy/hostinger`, copied `.env.production` without printing secrets, stopped `corvus_api` / `corvus_cron`, started `omen_api` / `omen_cron`, and verified public health/readiness. Primary deploy run `27995567629` then rebuilt/pushed Omen-only GHCR tags, pulled from `/opt/omen`, restarted `omen_api` / `omen_cron`, and passed workflow smoke. Tailscale fallback run `27996194346` also pulled/restarted from `/opt/omen` and passed smoke. Final public checks returned `status: ok`, `service: "omen-api"`, and readiness `ready`. GitHub repo renamed from `justinduverge-design/corvus` to `justinduverge-design/omen`, and local origin now points to `https://github.com/justinduverge-design/omen`. Retained Corvus references are compatibility (`/corvus`, `corvus.*` localStorage, `CORVUS_*` env fallbacks), history, or rollback artifacts (`/opt/corvus`, old GHCR tags, runner label `corvus-deploy`) pending an explicit cleanup window.
- **Phase 1.5g.1 motif hairline rule shipped.** The first Motif implementation keeps motifs as optional team-root data resolved from active palette roles, written to `--motif-*` tokens by `applyMotifTokens()`, and rendered only through explicit `[data-motif-target]` surfaces. The v1 renderer applies one active motif at a time and clears all motif tokens/attributes outside Team mode.
- **Omen-card exclusion is schema and CSS doctrine.** Every Phase 1.5g.1 motif must set `excludesOmenCard: true`; `assertCategoryShape()` enforces it, `test/teamMotifs.test.mjs` pins it, and no `data-motif-target="omen-card"` selector ships. Recommendation surfaces remain neutral across team themes.
- **Self-assessed hairlines only in v1.** PIT, MIA, NO, and GB ship static CSS hairlines with `trademarkReview: 'self-assessed'`. No logos, marks, corner ornaments, chevrons, diamonds, arrows, horseshoes, stars, fleur-de-lis, or SVG ornaments ship. Future mark-like motifs require `counsel-approved` before production render.
- **NO motif color changed to neutral cream for contrast.** The initial gold/purple direction failed the NO Mardi Gras special-surface motif contrast check; the shipped NO hairline resolves from `neutral`, which clears the motif threshold in both official and special variants.
- **Category validator becomes the forward gate for 1.5g.2/1.5g.3.** `frontend/src/lib/assertCategoryShape.js` now validates `motifs`, `typeFlourishes`, and `culturalMoments` in dev/test. It preserves the 1.5g.0 typography lock by rejecting italic-700 until the font import/self-host plan changes, and it rejects cultural moment scopes that include Omen or Trade recommendation surfaces.
- **Phase 1.5g.1 merged and deployed.** The scoped Phase 1.5g.1 commits were cherry-picked to `main` as `4efa338` (implementation) and `8e5d56b` (closeout) so unrelated generated `graphify-out/` output and logo assets from branch commit `86480b4` did not ship. `Deploy to Hostinger KVM1` run `28062382244` passed clean-runner quality, image build/push, KVM1 restart, workflow health smoke, and log tail. Independent canary passed root+`www` health/ready, homepage 200, Demo 200, expected 401 auth boundaries, HTTP->HTTPS redirect, and HSTS. Rollback target: revert `4efa338` + `8e5d56b` through a PR; the normal `main` workflow rebuilds and redeploys the prior images.

## Decisions Added 2026-06-25

- **`slops-graphify` v0.1.3 — SLOPS-root mode added; "fix the tool, not the layout."** Three nested git repos (L0 `dev/SLOPS/`, L1 `slops-saloon/`, L2 `slops-saloon/omen/`, the latter with its own origin `justinduverge-design/omen`) stay as-is — no submodule conversion, no repo restructuring. Instead, `slops-graphify` learned to walk past nested `.git/` boundaries when invoked at the SLOPS root. New runner at `Blueprints/skills/slops-graphify/slops_root_scan.py` auto-detects the SLOPS-root signature (path contains both `slops-saloon/` and `References/graphify/`), monkey-patches `graphify.detect._load_graphifyignore` *in-process* to drop ignore-pattern lines targeting nested git repos (no disk `.gitignore` mutation), runs `graphify.watch._rebuild_code` in one pass, and then runs a deterministic, no-LLM markdown-link enrichment pass that resolves `[text](path)` and inline-backtick `` `path.md` `` references against existing document nodes. Default behaviour is unchanged for non-SLOPS-root invocations; nested-repo recursion stays opt-in via auto-detect or explicit `--include-nested-repos`. Skill `SKILL.md` carries the explicit warning: "do not use this flag against an unrelated parent repo — it deliberately ignores the standard git boundary." Pass-2 canonical rebuild (`References/graphify/graphify-out/graph.json`) — 10,140 nodes / 10,743 edges / 792 communities; L0↔L1=39, L0↔L2=145 (≥27 baseline floor from 2026-06-21), L1↔L2=28. Closes the pass-1 carryover (which used `merge-graphs` and produced zero cross-layer edges). Report: `References/graphify/REFRESH_REPORT_2026-06-25-pass2.md`.
- **Phase 1.5g.2 — TypeFlourish schema + NE small-caps eyebrow shipped (branch `claude/corvus-kickoff-hre0je`, commit `440be1f`; not yet merged to `main`).** Mirrored the Motif system file-for-file: `typeFlourishes: []` on NE (`ne-engraved-eyebrow`, Alegreya Sans 600, `font-feature-settings: "smcp" 1`, tracking 0.12em, enabled in both Official and Special); new `frontend/src/lib/typeFlourishes.js` resolver (no trademark gate — typography isn't IP-sensitive the way motif geometry is); `teamTemplate.js` returns `typeFlourishes` sibling to `motifs`; `themeMode.js` adds `TYPE_FLOURISH_VARS` (6 vars) + `applyTypeFlourishTokens()` + `clearTypeFlourishTokens()`; `index.css` adds the `--type-flourish-*` defaults and `.type-flourish-eyebrow` utility; `Appearance.jsx`'s `cultureTag` pill drops fake-caps `uppercase` for true small-caps only when an `eyebrow`-scope flourish is active (NE only in v1). `assertCategoryShape()`'s `assertTypeFlourishes()` — built ahead-of-need in 1.5g.1 — needed no changes; it already rejects italic-700 and non-Alegreya families, which is the typography-lock gate this phase depended on.
- **Font-feature spike resolved — no self-host fallback needed.** Per the 1.5g.0 risk-carry-forward item (risk (d): "Google Fonts CSS2 OpenType feature retention is unverified at served-CSS level"), a Playwright/Chromium screenshot spike against the live Google Fonts CSS2 Alegreya Sans `@import` confirmed `font-feature-settings: "smcp" 1` renders visibly distinct small-caps glyphs (first letter full-height, subsequent letters reduced-height caps) versus `text-transform: uppercase` (all letters full-height). The spike script and screenshot live only in the session's `/tmp` scratchpad — they are not committed to the repo, so this verification is not independently re-runnable from the codebase alone. If that's later judged insufficient evidence, the spike should be re-run from a committed fixture.
- **Font-loading grep gate added.** `frontend/scripts/contrast-sweep.mjs` now runs `assertFontImportBaseline()` before the WCAG sweep: it fails if `frontend/src/index.css` has more than 2 `@import` lines or any `@font-face` line (current baseline), so a future self-hosting or new-font change requires a deliberate baseline bump rather than landing silently. Re-run confirmed no false positive against the unchanged Phase 1.4 imports.

Design-Done gate 3 (light/dark screenshots of the rendered `/account/appearance` cultureTag badge) was **not captured** — no dev server / browser-against-running-app path was available in this sandbox beyond the isolated font-feature spike page. New resolver `frontend/src/lib/culturalMoments.js`, calendar `frontend/src/data/nflCalendar.js`, the route-level mock/live indicator `frontend/src/lib/dataMode.js`, shared hook `frontend/src/lib/useActiveMoment.js`, and `frontend/src/components/layout/MomentChrome.jsx` (sibling of `<main>`). `themeMode.js` gains `MOMENT_VARS` + `applyMomentOverlay()`/`clearMomentTokens()`; `index.css` gains the moment defaults, the `color-mix` page-surface tint, and the `.moment-eyebrow` utility; Footer appends the moment citation without replacing the cultural anchor. `assertCulturalMoments` (built ahead-of-need in 1.5g.1) covered the data shape with no validator change.

Every other route leaves it `null` → suppressed. `/omen` and `/trade` resolve to an empty scope set, so the recommendation surfaces never receive moment chrome even in mock mode. Tint is further limited to `/account`, `/football`, `/ledger` per the page-system Motif/Moment posture; `/account/appearance` and `/standings` are eyebrow-only.

## Decisions Added 2026-06-27

- **Phase 2.17 last-result contract is fantasy-matchup based, fail-closed, and kickoff-null in v1.** `/api/dashboard/summary.platforms.{sleeper,yahoo,espn}` now exposes `lastResult`, `lastGameId`, and `lastGameKickoff` for the previous regular-season week when a connected platform can safely determine the user's prior fantasy matchup outcome. Ties, unavailable data, week 1, provider errors, or ambiguous parser state return nulls rather than guessed wins/losses. `lastGameKickoff` is kept in the contract but remains null because Sleeper/Yahoo/ESPN fantasy matchup resources used here do not expose a real kickoff timestamp. A later true-kickoff requirement needs a separate schedule-source decision.
- **ESPN last-result uses the existing high-risk private adapter only, with no new credential surface.** The implementation reuses existing Vault-backed ESPN credential flow and the adapter's v3 request family; it returns only normalized `W`/`L`/id/null fields. No raw ESPN cookie values, Vault ids, auth headers, or raw ESPN bodies are returned. Real ESPN production smoke remains Justin-gated.
- **Phase 3.12 KVM2 Gemma bridge is private-only and fail-closed.** `LLM_BASE_URL` is now accepted only when its host is `localhost`, private IPv4/IPv6, Tailscale CGNAT `100.64.0.0/10`, or a private-style DNS suffix such as `.internal`, `.local`, `.tailnet`, or `.ts.net`. Public/invalid URLs are reported as safe status (`misconfigured_public` / `invalid_url`) on `/api/ready` and `/api/platform-status`, but the URL is never returned and calls fall back to deterministic/template narration. No `.env`, deploy config, package files, KVM/Tailscale state, Supabase, Stripe, or production behavior changed. Default local model target is now `gemma4:e2b-q4_0`; `LLM_MODEL` still overrides it.
- **Phase 3.12 merged and deployed.** PR #70 squash-merged to `main` as `a13160b`; Deploy to Hostinger KVM1 run `28306784898` passed quality, GHCR image build/push, KVM1 pull/restart, workflow health smoke, and independent canary. Live `/api/ready` and `/api/platform-status` report `llm.status: configured_private`, `llm_private: true`, `public_url_exposed: false`, and no URL/host/port. Production model is `gemma3:4b` because the KVM1 env override wins over the new code default; this is expected. Rollback target: revert `a13160b` through a PR so the normal `main` workflow rebuilds and redeploys prior images.

## Decisions Added 2026-06-29

- **Phase 1.5d post-win pulse ships as single-win behavior only.** The `/football` pulse consumes the existing dashboard platform `lastResult` / `lastGameId` contract. It shows a factual `<Team> W - bright today` chip, plays one 800ms team-accent header-rule wash once per stored `lastGameId`, and lifts the embedded standings current-user row tint from 14% to 22% while the latest connected platform reports a win. It does not infer streaks, project future wins, or change recommendation behavior.
- **Win-streak rewards require backend truth before production UI.** The preferred hybrid path is documented in `Blueprints/specs/page-system.md`: 1 win = Bright today, 2 = Heating up, 3 = On a streak, 4 = The omen favors you, 5+ = Crowned run. Production streak rewards are blocked until `GET /api/dashboard/summary` exposes a backend-computed `currentWinStreak`-style field derived from real provider matchup history. Browser-local history may not be used to present a real streak.
- **Two parallel sessions built Phase 2.17 independently; reconciled by layering, not replacing.** A Claude session picked up Phase 2.17 from `agent_inbox.md` on a stale local `main` that predated a Codex session's already-merged implementation (PR #69, `8331b72`/`c0dc5e7`/`d86de57`, see `sprints_completed.md` 2026-06-27 entry); the frontend (Phase 1.5d) was already built against Codex's live contract. Discovered via `mergeStateStatus: DIRTY` when checking the Claude PR's (#73) mergeability before merge — caught before anything reached `main`. Justin reviewed both diffs and resolved: keep the calendar-math week determination already in `getCurrentNflWeekContext()` (no live ESPN-schedule-completion polling), keep Codex's structure as-is, and layer Claude's two genuine improvements on top of it. PR #73 closed as superseded; the improvements shipped as PR #74.
- **Phase 2.17 follow-up — Redis caching + request timeout merged and deployed.** PR #74 squash-merged to `main` as `05bb7e8`. Adds: (1) 6h Redis caching of completed-week `lastResult` lookups for Sleeper/Yahoo/ESPN — a finished week's matchup outcome never changes, so this avoids refetching on every dashboard summary request; ESPN's cache key is `leagueId:week:teamId` only, never the SWID cookie, and caching is skipped when `teamId` is unknown. (2) a 4s timeout (`withTimeout`/`Promise.race`) around each platform lookup in `dashboard.js`, since Yahoo's `fetch` and ESPN's `https.request` have no native timeout and a hung call could otherwise stall the whole response. `Deploy to Hostinger KVM1` run `28404193238` passed quality (398/399 — 1 pre-existing CRLF-only failure in `deployHardening.test.js` unrelated to this diff), build, and KVM1 deploy; post-deploy `/api/health` and `/api/ready` both confirmed live with `redis: true`. Rollback target: revert `05bb7e8`.

## Decisions Added 2026-06-30

- **Phase 1.7 platform colors: deepen-for-margin over knife-edge brand accuracy.** ESPN's most-cited public brand red (`#E52534`) only clears 4.5:1 white-text AA contrast by ~0.02 once paired with this app's actual off-white text token (`#F5F0E8`, not pure white) — too thin a margin to ship as a button-fill color across both themes. Deepened to `#C81E2C` (~5:1+ margin) for both themes rather than ship a value that passes the bare formula but fails the moment any adjacent token shifts slightly. Yahoo's sourced `#410093` and the kept Sleeper approximation didn't need this treatment (large existing margins). Sleeper has no confirmed official public hex; the pre-existing approximate blue was kept rather than substituting an unverified guess — flagged for Justin to correct if he has Sleeper's actual brand kit.
- **Platform brand color split into two token families, not one.** Yahoo's and ESPN's brand hues are saturated enough that the same hex which works as a button-fill background (paired with white/near-white text) is too low-luminance to read as small text directly on the dark-theme's near-black surface. Rather than pick one compromise value that's marginal in both contexts, added dark-theme-only `-chip` override tokens (lightened ~50% toward white) used only for small badge/icon text; button fills keep the brand-accurate base token. Sleeper's blue didn't need a chip override — it's bright enough for both contexts already.
- **`deployHardening.test.js`'s 1 failing assertion is confirmed pre-existing, not Phase-1.7-caused.** Already flagged as "1 pre-existing CRLF-only failure... unrelated to this diff" in the 2026-06-29 Phase 2.17-follow-up entry above (`05bb7e8`) — reproduced again here in isolation against a file (`.github/workflows/deploy.yml`) untouched by this session's diff. Spun off as its own background-task suggestion (CRLF-vs-LF root cause in the workflow file or the test's regex) rather than fixed under Phase 1.7, since it's unrelated to platform colors and pre-dates this session by at least one prior phase.
- **Phase 1.7 committed and pushed to `main` on Justin's explicit request.** Commit `ea756b1`. Touching `frontend/src/**` falls outside `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`'s `paths-ignore` list, so the push auto-triggered `Deploy to Hostinger KVM1` run `28414186836` — quality, build, and deploy all green. Independently re-verified post-deploy via `curl`: `/api/health` → `status: ok`; `/api/ready` → `status: ready`; homepage → `200`. Rollback target: revert `ea756b1` through a PR so the normal `main` workflow rebuilds and redeploys prior images.
- **Phase 1.8 confidence gradient uses CSS-native `color-mix(in hsl, ...)` over a hand-rolled JS HSL interpolator.** Two fixed tokens (`--color-confidence-floor` crimson, `--color-confidence-ceiling` deep green) plus the browser's own shorter-arc hue interpolation produce the required amber midpoint automatically — no third "midpoint" token needed. Matches the existing `platformChip.js`/`positionChip.js` convention of doing color math via `color-mix()` in CSS custom properties rather than computing hex values in JS. Verified via direct browser `color-mix` evaluation (not visual screenshot): floor `#701020` → `rgb(112,16,32)`, midpoint at 50% → `rgb(105,112,24)` (reads amber/gold), ceiling `#206F3A` → `rgb(32,111,58)`, consistent across both `data-theme` values.
- **Confidence floor color was brightened mid-build after contrast math, not shipped as first-drafted.** Initial draft `#5B1010` measured 1.24:1 relative-luminance contrast against the dark `--color-surface-1` track — barely distinguishable at low scores. Revised to `#701020` (1.45:1) before closing the task. Still below WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1 non-text guideline; accepted as a documented tradeoff (not silently passed) because both `OmenOfTheWeek.jsx` and `DraftAssistant.jsx` always print the numeric score and label as text alongside the bar — satisfying brand-system's "risk/confidence carry text labels, not color alone" rule, which is the actual accessibility backstop here. Self-administered `slops-ui-ux-audit` verdict: merge, P1 polish note (non-text contrast headroom on the crimson end and borderline on the green end at 2.75:1), no P0. Full numbers in `Blueprints/specs/page-system.md` Confidence Gradient (Phase 1.8) — Resolved section.
- **`ui-ux-pro-max`'s `scripts/search.py` is not actually present in this environment** — only `SKILL.md` exists under `~/.claude/skills/ui-ux-pro-max/`, no `scripts/` directory. Recorded as a procedure gap (skill invoked, produced no result) rather than claimed as evidence; `slops-ui-ux-audit` (Slops-native, no missing-asset issue) supplied the actual verdict instead.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-01

- **Two sessions (Claude, this one; Codex, concurrently) independently built Phase 1.9 metallic tier treatment on the same working tree.** Both landed on the same helper design and the same footer-exclusion scope, so there was no functional conflict — but both wrote overlapping close-out docs (this log, `current_sprint.md`, `agent_inbox.md`, `skill-usage-ledger.md`, `done/LEDGER.md`) and duplicate handoff/audit files (`Blueprints/handoffs/2026-07-01-phase1-9-metallic-tier-treatment.md` (Claude) vs. `...-handoff.md` (Codex); `Blueprints/audits/2026-07-01-phase1-9-metallic-tier-{code-review,ui-ux-audit}.md` (Codex only)). Flagged to Justin; no attempt made to silently merge or delete either agent's artifacts. Real gap: the loop has no lock/claim step, so two agents can pull the same unpinned top-5 item in the same window. Worth a `slops-retro` pass on `agent_inbox.md`'s auto-populate mechanic (e.g. a claimed-by marker) if this recurs.
- **Phase 1.9 metallic tier treatment scoped to the card-header ordinal pill only.** `page-system.md`'s "Metallic Tier (Phase 1.9)" entry says "Draft Assistant top-3 ordinal pills" (singular concept), and Approved Pattern #1 explicitly locks the "ADP / Omen #N footer" as unchanged structure. An earlier working-tree pass had also applied the new `metallicTierStyle()` helper to the separate `Omen #N` ADP-footer badge in `AdpRow`; reverted that scope-creep back to the plain badge so only `RecommendationCard`'s `rec.rank` circle carries the metallic treatment, matching the locked structure doctrine. `page-system.md`'s Resolved note corrected to match.
- **Metallic tier tokens are theme-invariant, like the Phase 1.8 confidence tokens.** `--color-tier-gold` (`#D4AF37`), `--color-tier-silver` (`#C0C0C0`), `--color-tier-bronze` (`#8C5A2B`) and their `--color-on-tier-*` text-color pairs are identical across `:root`, `[data-theme="dark"]`, and `[data-theme="light"]` — they're decorative/branded metals, not page-surface tokens that should shift with light/dark mode (same reasoning as the confidence-gradient floor/ceiling tokens).
- **Contrast math (hand-computed WCAG relative luminance, same method as Phase 1.7/1.8):** gold `#D4AF37` vs. black text ≈9.99:1; silver `#C0C0C0` vs. black text ≈11.55:1; bronze `#8C5A2B` vs. off-white (`#F5F0E8`) text ≈5.12:1 (vs. only ≈3.6:1 with black text, which is why bronze gets a distinct on-color). All three clear WCAG AA (4.5:1) comfortably. The rank number is also redundant with the card's visual order and the separate `Omen #N` footer text, so this badge is decorative reinforcement, not the sole means of conveying rank.
- **Gradient + bevel implemented via CSS `color-mix()`, no JS color math** — mirrors the `lib/confidenceGradient.js` / `lib/platformChip.js` precedent of doing color math in CSS custom properties rather than computing hex in JS. `frontend/src/lib/metallicTier.js`'s `metallicTierStyle(rank)` returns a `linear-gradient(135deg, lighter-tint 0%, base 45%, darker-shade 100%)` background plus an inset `box-shadow` bevel (light highlight top-left, dark shadow bottom-right); returns `null` for rank 4+ so those keep the existing plain bordered circle unchanged.
- **`.claude/launch.json` added** so `preview_start`/`preview_screenshot` can drive the frontend dev server (`npm --prefix frontend run dev`, port 5173) for visual QA in this and future sessions. Not deploy config, not a package file, not a secret — a local dev-tool convenience file.
- **Not committed.** Per the global "never commit unless explicitly asked" rule, Phase 1.9's diff is left in the working tree; a commit/push needs Justin's explicit go-ahead.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-02

- **Phase 1.12 gray-contrast work was scoped to live routes, not retired Hall of Records doctrine.** By implementation time, the older `/hall-of-records` username-column note in `page-system.md` and `current_sprint.md` no longer mapped to a real user-visible surface: Hall of Records had already been retired to `/ledger`, and the live Ledger/Move History UI exposes no username column. The build closed the actual remaining contrast issues on `/account/appearance`, `/standings`, and `/onboarding`, then marked the Hall of Records target as documentation debt rather than inventing a fake replacement UI just to satisfy an old checklist line.
- **Phase 1.12 used existing text tokens only — no new gray palette was added.** The contrast pass promoted the `/account/appearance` intro paragraph and `/onboarding` success copy from `--color-text-secondary` to `--color-text-primary`, and promoted `/standings` W-L / PF / PA data from lower-contrast gray usage to existing AA-safe text tokens (`--color-text-secondary` for non-current rows, `--color-text-primary` for the highlighted current-user row). This kept the fix inside the existing design system rather than adding one-off color values.
- **Standings current-user emphasis is now structural, not just chromatic.** The full `/standings` page's current-user row now uses a stronger team-accent surface tint plus a 4px team-accent left edge, with the `you` label rendered as explicit accent text. That change makes the row identifiable even before reading the record columns, and it no longer depends on the prior too-subtle underline-style treatment described in older QA notes.
- **Phase 1.13 committed as a scoped branch, not directly onto local `main`.** Local `main` was already 2 commits ahead of `origin/main` from Codex's uncommitted-to-remote Phase 1.12 work when this session started. Rather than adding a third unbranched commit onto that drift, this session branched `frontend/phase1-13-mobile-qa-sweep` from current local `main` per `slops-git-flow` and committed the scoped code change there (`f826d2e`). Push/PR/merge remain Justin's gate for all three phases' commits.
- **React 18's `inert` attribute needs the empty-string idiom, not a bare boolean.** `inert={!open}` on a DOM element triggers React's "non-boolean attribute" warning in React 18.3 (native boolean coercion for `inert` landed in React 19). The working pattern is `inert={open ? undefined : ''}` — `undefined` omits the attribute entirely, empty string sets `inert=""` which the browser treats as present regardless of value. Applied to both `Header.jsx`'s `NavDrawer` and `HelpButton.jsx`'s Help panel, which shared the same pre-existing bug: closed slide-in panels stayed keyboard-focusable off-screen via `translateX` with no `inert`/`aria-hidden` guard. Worth checking any future slide-in/off-canvas panel for the same gap.
- **`:focus-visible` does not reliably fire for scripted `.focus()` calls on `<button>` elements in automated browser testing.** Confirmed by testing an already-shipped, previously-audited focus-visible pattern (the Draft Assistant number inputs) — `<input>` elements show the expected outline under scripted focus, but `<button>` elements don't, matching the real browser heuristic where mouse-origin/programmatic focus doesn't trigger `:focus-visible` on buttons (only genuine keyboard-origin focus does) while text inputs always show it. Do not conclude a button is missing its focus ring from a scripted-focus `getComputedStyle` check alone — verify the CSS class matches an already-proven pattern, or simulate a real Tab keypress.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-03 (Phase 1.13 remediation)

- **Helmet's `upgrade-insecure-requests` CSP default broke the SPA outright over plain HTTP in real Safari.** `useDefaults: true` silently merges this directive in unless explicitly excluded per-key. Since `src/server.js` terminates TLS at the Nginx/Oracle LB (`trust proxy` set to `1`), the Node process only ever speaks plain HTTP — including for direct local/LAN QA — and WebKit's enforcement of that directive (rewriting every same-origin asset fetch to `https://`) fails with nothing on TLS at that port, so the app never mounted. Fixed with a single explicit `upgradeInsecureRequests: null` in `src/middleware/security.js`, which Helmet 7.2.0 treats as excluding just that one default (confirmed via `normalizeDirectives()` source) — all other CSP hardening defaults stay on. Verified end-to-end against the real production-mode server on `:3000` with real `webdriver.Safari()`. This touches `src/middleware/security.js`, which sits near this repo's hard-boundary line (security headers, not auth/secrets/deploy config) — done under the user's explicit "act in the two findings" instruction, not pulled unprompted. Full writeup: [`Blueprints/handoffs/2026-07-03-phase1-13-remediation-csp-and-focus-trap.md`](../Blueprints/handoffs/2026-07-03-phase1-13-remediation-csp-and-focus-trap.md).
- **The nav drawer / Help panel "missing focus trap" finding was reframed mid-investigation: it's compounded by a macOS Safari default-mode quirk, not just a missing trap.** Diagnostic scripts showed the drawer's interactive elements were in correct DOM order and `.focus()`-able, yet real Safari `Tab` skipped them entirely — because Safari's default keyboard mode (no "Full Keyboard Access") excludes plain `<button>`/`<a href>` elements from native Tab order unless they carry an explicit `tabindex` attribute. A trap that only guards the first/last element (relying on native traversal for interior hops) would still fail in Safari, since interior hops between un-indexed links are exactly what gets skipped. Built `frontend/src/lib/useFocusTrap.js`, which intercepts **every** Tab/Shift+Tab keypress while active and drives focus via `.focus()` (which bypasses the Safari restriction entirely) rather than letting any native traversal happen inside the container. Wired into both `Header.jsx`'s `NavDrawer` and `HelpButton.jsx`'s `HelpPanel`. Verified with real Safari WebDriver: 12+ forward and reverse Tab hops cycle correctly inside each panel with zero escapes, across multiple full wrap cycles. `qa_suite.py`'s `check3_panels` — the one failing check in the original Phase 1.13 sweep — now passes.
- **Both fixes verified with real Safari WebDriver, not simulated/scripted DOM events, matching Phase 1.13's original QA methodology.** Neither commit has been made — changes sit unstaged on `frontend/phase1-13-mobile-qa-sweep` pending Justin's explicit commit go-ahead, per standing instruction.
