## Decisions Added 2026-07-02 (Phase 1.13 mobile QA sweep — audit)

- **Phase 1.13 mobile QA sweep — audit half complete, via a new method (Cowork + Safari WebDriver), not the `mobile-first-qa-playbook` skill's default emulated-viewport approach.** Run against production `https://slopssaloon.com` using real desktop Safari (WebKit — same engine as iOS Safari) driven by Selenium/`safaridriver`, resized to iPhone SE/15 Pro/15 Pro Max widths, rather than browser dev-tools device emulation. Chosen because Chrome's extension-based automation wasn't connected in that session and WebKit-native testing is closer to a real iPhone than Chrome/Blink emulation would have been anyway. 298 screenshots covering all 14 routes × 3 widths, plus all 32 NFL team color schemes clicked through the real Appearance-page UI (not synthetic state) across the 8 accent-active pages. Full findings: `Solutions/reports/2026-07-02-mobile-qa-omen.md`; screenshots: `Solutions/reports/_screenshots/2026-07-02-mobile-qa/`.
- **No P0s found.** The mobile-first layout work (Phase 1.3 onward) holds up across every route, width, and team theme — no overflow, no clipped text, no undersized touch targets detected.
- **Two P1s surfaced, neither triaged or fixed yet (audit only, per the skill's own "does not execute fixes" scope):** (1) League Standings fails to load on `/standings` and `/football` for a fully-connected account, reproduced across every viewport and many team themes — open question for engineering whether this is a genuine bug or an off-season empty state wearing error-style copy ("Couldn't load... Try again"); (2) an unhandled 429 rate-limit response renders raw backend JSON directly on the page with no styling — no generic API-error boundary exists anywhere in the frontend. The 429 was very likely triggered by this test's own rapid request volume (32 team switches in quick succession), not organic user behavior, but the missing error-handling is real regardless of trigger.
- **Team-theming re-verification: the Phase 1.5e "6 teams flip to light axis" plan referenced in `current_sprint.md`/older docs is stale relative to the shipped Phase 1.5h multi-palette system.** `nflTeams.js` now derives light/dark per-team from a `surfaceRole` field per named palette (not a fixed light/dark team list) — confirmed working end-to-end: e.g. MIA's official palette (`surfaceRole: 'neutral'` → light beach-sand `#FDF8F2`) correctly renders a light page, matching the doctrine comment in `nflTeams.js` ("page is light when the team's surface color is light"), not the older axis-flip plan.
- **Methodology note for future sessions testing this app's theme system:** setting `omen.theme.mode`/`omen.theme.team` directly via `localStorage` does not reliably stick — something in the app re-syncs the account's saved `favorite_team` preference over a raw injection. Driving the actual Appearance-page team tiles via UI clicks (which calls the real `setThemeTeam()` + `PATCH /api/account/preferences` handler) is the reliable path.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-04 (P1 triage — rate-limit scope + Standings off-season)

- **The unhandled-429 P1 was a rate-limiter scoping bug in `src/server.js`, not a missing frontend error boundary.** Every current call site (`Standings.jsx`, `LeagueStandings.jsx`, `Ledger.jsx`/`MoveHistory.jsx`) already caught fetch errors gracefully via the shared `apiFetch()` client before this session touched anything. The general rate limiter (100 req/min) was mounted globally ahead of the static-asset middleware and the SPA catch-all, so every JS/CSS/image/font request and every hard-navigation page load counted against the same budget as API calls — a few page loads or the audit's own 32-team-switch sweep could trip it, and a rate-limited full-page navigation renders the raw JSON body directly since React never gets a chance to mount. Justin approved touching `src/server.js`'s rate-limit mount point specifically (this sits near, but is distinct from, `src/middleware/security.js`, which a prior session flagged as needing explicit go-ahead) before the edit was made.
- **Fix scoped the limiter to `/api/*` only, verified live rather than assumed.** Booted the production-mode server locally (`node -r dotenv/config src/server.js`, port 3000) via a new `.claude/launch.json` `backend` entry and fired 150 rapid requests at a static asset plus 150 at `/api/dashboard/summary`: static asset 150/150 → 200 (zero 429s, confirming exemption); API route 100 → 401 then 50 → 429 (confirming the limiter is still fully functional for actual API traffic, not disabled). `/api/health` still bypasses as before. Full `npm test` 402/402 (no regressions — the previously-documented pre-existing CRLF failure in `test/deployHardening.test.js` is also gone, resolved by a concurrent Codex Phase 1.14 session, not this change). `npm audit --audit-level=moderate` 0; `git diff --check` clean.
- **The Standings-fails-to-load P1 is confirmed to be the audit's hypothesis (b): a mislabeled off-season case, not a frontend copy bug.** `Standings.jsx` already has a distinct, correctly-written empty state ("No standings yet — Standings will appear once the season begins") that only renders on a *successful* response with `standings: []`. `src/routes/league.js`'s `GET /standings` (lines 192-222) has no off-season detection — any provider-call exception (plausible in July with no live matchups) falls through to a generic 502 `league_standings_provider_failed`, which the frontend correctly renders as its *error* state instead. `getCurrentNflWeekContext()` (`src/services/nflSchedule.js`) has no off-season boolean to short-circuit on.
- **Not fixed inline — routed as one combined backend request instead of a standalone stopgap, per Justin's explicit direction.** This is the same gap already open since 2026-06-25 (Phase 1.10B) for the dashboard route's `omen_of_the_week.status`. Rather than hack a second, independently-invented off-season check into `league.js`, filed a combined request in `Blueprints/handoffs/frontend-to-backend.md` recommending one shared `isOffSeason()`-style helper both routes call, so the two surfaces can't disagree about when the season has started. Full triage + evidence: `Blueprints/handoffs/2026-07-04-ratelimit-scope-and-standings-offseason-triage.md`.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-04 (Phase 2.9 account delete UI)

- **Phase 2.9 account deletion copy says "Omen data", not "account" or provider data.** The mounted route is `src/routes/userPrivacy.js` at `DELETE /api/user/delete`; it deletes Omen-side rows and attempts Vault cleanup, but it does not delete Yahoo/Sleeper/ESPN-held data or sign-in-provider data. The UI therefore uses "Delete Omen data" and explicitly excludes fantasy platforms and sign-in providers.
- **The destructive confirmation phrase is locked to `DELETE MY OMEN DATA`.** The stale backend-to-frontend handoff phrase `DELETE MY CORVUS DATA` was corrected, and the new frontend helper/test pins the exact Omen phrase so future UI copy does not drift back to Corvus or to the legacy `src/omen_gdpr.js` phrase.
- **Phase 2.9 was isolated from the off-season signal branch.** Work was branched from `origin/main` as `codex/phase2-9-account-delete-ui` rather than stacking on `codex/canonical-offseason-signal`, because Justin approved the branching recommendation and the two scopes should review separately. A temporary inbox-claim stash from the original branch remains untouched.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-04 (Phase 2.10 trade share card)

- **Trade share UI creates snapshots from the last submitted analyzer payload, not from unsaved form edits.** After `POST /api/trade/compare` succeeds, `/trade` stores the exact normalized send/receive payload that produced the visible result. The Share result control posts that stored payload to `POST /api/trade/share`, so a user who edits the form after seeing a result cannot accidentally share a different, uncomputed trade.
- **Public share pages are snapshot surfaces, not live provider surfaces.** `/trade/share/:hash` reads `GET /api/trade/share/:hash`, labels the data as a public snapshot, and explicitly says it does not include connected-platform context, ESPN cookies, tokens, or private league data. It does not call provider routes, Supabase user data, auth, or LLM narration.
- **Server-side OG rendering uses SVG to avoid a new image-rendering dependency.** `GET /api/trade/share/:hash/og.svg` renders a bounded 1200x630 SVG from the sanitized public snapshot and escapes XML. This keeps the phase dependency-free and avoids adding a headless browser/canvas service for one share card. Crawler meta for `/trade/share/:hash` points `og:image`/`twitter:image` to this endpoint.
- **Confidence score on the share card is a display mapping from the existing backend confidence label.** The current backend snapshot exposes `low`/`medium` confidence labels, not a 0-100 scalar. The public card maps `low` -> 45, `medium` -> 70, and `high` -> 85 for compact readability while keeping the label visible beside the score. If backend later returns a numeric confidence, that should replace the mapping.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-08 (Phase 4.20a mobile-build kill-switch)

- **`VITE_APP_STORE_BUILD` is frontend-render-only; no backend gating added.** The sprint item allowed splitting into a `4.20a-backend` follow-up if server-side gating was needed. Mirrored the existing `VITE_ESPN_ENABLED` precedent instead: that flag already hides the ESPN connect UI without gating the backend `/api/platforms/espn/connect` route, and that posture has stood as the accepted pattern (a user could still hit the route directly, same as today). Since the done-when only requires UI-entry-point absence, no backend route/schema change was made and 4.20a-backend was not triggered.
- **The new flag is declared per-file (`const APP_STORE_BUILD = import.meta.env.VITE_APP_STORE_BUILD === 'true';`), not via a shared config module.** This matches the existing duplication convention for `BILLING_ENABLED`/`ESPN_ENABLED` across `Account.jsx`, `UpgradeState.jsx`, `PlatformConnections.jsx`, and `ConnectLeague.jsx`. A shared module would be the only cross-cutting import among otherwise-independent files, for no functional gain — `grep -rn VITE_APP_STORE_BUILD frontend/src` already gives full cross-file visibility, and the new regression test (`test/appStoreBuildKillSwitch.test.js`) pins every gate site so drift can't reopen silently.
- **Two existing OR-bypasses had to be closed, not just the primary flags gated.** `PlatformConnections.jsx`'s `showEspnRow = ESPN_ENABLED || espnRecovery` would have let a `?recovery=espn_*` deep link resurrect the ESPN row in an app-store build; `ConnectLeague.jsx`'s `EspnCard` had `if (!ESPN_ENABLED && !connected) return null;`, which would have let a previously-connected ESPN user still see the card. Both now short-circuit on `APP_STORE_BUILD` before the bypass condition is evaluated.
- **CI/deploy build-arg wiring for the new flag is deferred, not built.** `VITE_ESPN_ENABLED` has `ARG`/`ENV` passthrough in the current web deploy pipeline because it ships in that build; `VITE_APP_STORE_BUILD` is a kill-switch for a mobile wrapper build that doesn't exist in this repo's CI yet, so wiring it into the Hostinger web deploy would be premature/wrong scope. Flagged as a natural follow-up once actual mobile packaging (e.g. Capacitor) is built — not a silently-closed gap.
- **Backfilled the missing `VITE_BILLING_ENABLED` doc entry alongside this task.** It's read in code (`Account.jsx`, `UpgradeState.jsx`) but was never documented in `.env.example` or `deploy/hostinger/ENV-INVENTORY.md`. Added as a one-line adjacent fix while editing the same doc section, not a separate scoped task.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-08 (Phase 4.20c reviewer/demo access playbook)

- **`/demo` is the answer, not a new reviewer-only surface.** Considered whether a dedicated reviewer/QA account or a new backend fixture route was needed; neither is — the existing public, no-auth `/demo` route already provides a genuinely computed (not static) recommendation via the real optimizer fed a deterministic fixture, with zero Supabase/platform/LLM calls. Building anything new would duplicate `Blueprints/demo-mode.md`'s existing contract for no benefit.
- **The authenticated dashboard's live-recommendation gate is documented as a real limitation, not worked around.** `Onboarding.jsx`'s `ConnectStep` has no skip option and no seeded demo backend account exists. The playbook explicitly tells reviewers/agents not to attempt a `localStorage['omen.onboarding.done']` devtools bypass — that's an unsupported hack, and presenting it as a documented flow would misrepresent what's actually shippable/reviewable.
- **Verification substituted direct source reading and isolated function execution for a live server click-through**, because root `npm ci` fails in this sandbox on a pre-existing lockfile/dependency mismatch (`Missing: gcp-metadata@7.0.1`, unrelated to this task) and plain `npm install` was avoided since it would touch the lockfile without approval. `buildDemoModeResponse()` was executed directly via `node -e` (no Express/Supabase dependency needed) and returned `state: "success"` with the exact banner/notice text documented in the playbook. Disclosed as a known limitation in the sprint closeout, not silently skipped.
- **`Blueprints/demo-mode.md`'s stale status line is discovered, not fixed.** Its header still says "frontend `/demo` implementation pending," but the frontend shipped in Phase 2.7 (2026-06-19). Left uncorrected since it's outside this task's "Docs affected: new playbook file" scope; filed as a small follow-up item in `Direction/agent_inbox.md`'s Top 5 instead of silently patching a file this task wasn't scoped to touch.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-12 (Phase 4.20d store metadata/gambling copy audit)

- **Reused the Phase 4.16 packet's already-approved general attribution clause verbatim, rather than inventing new disclaimer wording.** `Legal/2026-06-28-open-agreements-provider-paragraphs.md` is marked review-only/not published, but its general clause ("Platform trademarks belong to their respective owners. Omen is not endorsed by or affiliated with those platforms.") is legally-reviewed content, not draft prose. Reusing it verbatim on the two highest-severity surfaces (Onboarding's platform-picker step, README's platform/status tables) closes the disclosure gap without publishing the full packet or waiting on a separate copy-approval cycle.
- **`Landing.jsx`'s waitlist platform picker (`PLATFORMS = ['ESPN','Yahoo','Sleeper','Not sure yet']`) was judged not a claims surface.** It's a form-input option list for "which platform do you use," not a marketing statement about data access, support level, or live-ness — so it doesn't trigger the same attribution requirement as a support-status claim.
- **"Live" wording describing Omen's own compute (Landing.jsx's "run the live Trade Analyzer," README's data-status table) was left as-is rather than softened.** These already correctly distinguish Omen's own live computation from third-party platform data claims, consistent with the mock-vs-live labeling rule in `facts-of-record.md`; softening them would reduce honesty, not risk.
- **No gambling/betting/DFS/odds/wagering/guaranteed-winnings language was found in any audited surface** — confirmed via full-file review, not just grep, since marketing copy like "See the winning move" needed a human read to rule out outcome-guarantee framing (judged acceptable: competitive/decision framing, not a wagering claim).

## Decisions Added 2026-07-12 (Justin — batch decision review on open blockers)

- **ESPN public/mobile posture: ESPN stays available on mobile via a browser-extension referral, not native cookie capture.** Rather than hiding ESPN entirely on mobile (Phase 4.20a's current posture) or blocking it behind counsel sign-off, Justin wants an in-app onboarding path: link the user to the existing browser-extension cookie flow, plus onboarding text/email instructions on how to connect ESPN from mobile if needed. Tracked as a new build item: "ESPN mobile-connect onboarding" in `current_sprint.md` Backend — Behind launch readiness.
- **Stripe is out of Omen entirely — not just web-only.** Justin: the app will be completely free; Stripe will not be used on this product at all (he uses Stripe on other projects, not Omen). This supersedes the earlier "Stripe stays web-only, mobile ships free" framing with a full-removal target. Removal itself is not yet authorized to build — it touches the payment Do Not Touch boundary — so a new Tech Debt item was filed requesting Justin confirm exact removal scope (UI-only vs. full backend/schema removal) before any code changes.
- **`AI_PROVIDER=local|cloud` toggle dollar cap: $0.** Justin: it's a local model, so the cap is $0 — i.e., cloud AI spend is not authorized at all under this toggle; local-only. This satisfies Phase 3.15's blocking requirement ("DO NOT BUILD until the cap is logged with Justin's approved dollar figure"). Phase 3.15 is now unblocked to build, with the explicit constraint that any `cloud` provider path must hard-fail or stay disabled unless a future decision raises the cap above $0.
- **Tuesday scoring enablement: approved to proceed.** Justin wants `OMEN_CRON_SCORING_ENABLED` enabled and added to the active sprint queue. The existing technical gate (approved Supabase dry-run validation immediately before the flip) still applies — this decision removes the founder-approval blocker, not the dry-run step itself.
- **Phase 4.20b (public legal/support pages) and Phase 4.16 (legal packet): approved to publish, pending a final wording verification pass.** Justin is ready to publish; he wants the wording double-checked first, not re-drafted from scratch. Phase 4.20b's blocked-by was changed from "Justin/counsel sign-off" to "none," with the wording-verification pass folded into that task rather than treated as a separate gate. Also flagged during this pass: the packet's Data Sharing paragraph lists "payment... providers" among Omen's service providers — should be removed or qualified given Stripe is not used on this product.
- **Marketing pillars: leave as provisional, no action.** Justin chose to leave the four brand-board pillars (DETECT THE SIGNAL / ANALYZE THE DATA / PREDICT THE OUTCOME / WIN WITH CONFIDENCE) parked as-is in `Brand/brand-system.md` §10a rather than lock, revise, or replace them now. Still not usable in marketing/ads/in-app copy until a future explicit close-out.
- **`mvp-move` optimizer: merge into Omen, take the best of both.** Justin chose the merge option over keeping `POST /api/optimizer/mvp-move` as a separate Pro enrichment layer. The `getOmenOfTheWeekMock()` retire-vs-fallback decision and the recovery-analytics ship-timing decision are folded into that same merge design pass rather than decided independently, since both depend on what the merged recommendation contract ends up looking like.
- **Retiring the black-background logo fallback: clarified, not yet actionable.** Justin didn't recognize "prod soak window" framing — clarified in the sprint item that this means confirming ~1-2 stable weeks of production traffic after Phase 4.21 (the actual asset swap) ships, before deleting the old fallback PNG. Phase 4.21 itself hasn't landed yet, so this decision is really "do Phase 4.21 first" — added to the sprint as a dated, sequenced follow-up rather than a standalone ambiguous blocker.
- **Trade Analyzer Buy-Low mock refresh: build the real endpoint.** Justin chose building `GET /api/trade/pulse` now over a manual seasonal-refresh checklist stopgap. Tracked as a new build item in `current_sprint.md` Backend — Behind launch readiness, reusing the existing Phase 2 Request 20 contract shape from `Blueprints/handoffs/frontend-to-backend.md`.
- **Sleeper's brand hex: could not be independently verified this session.** Justin asked whether the color could be pulled from Sleeper's live website. Attempted via `WebFetch` to sleeper.com and a Wikipedia lookup; both were rejected at the network-proxy level (`gateway answered 403 to CONNECT`, confirmed via `$HTTPS_PROXY/__agentproxy/status`) — this environment's outbound network policy blocks arbitrary external domains, not something a future agent should assume is fixable by retrying. The existing approximation (`--color-platform-sleeper: #1FA3E8`, dark-mode `#0E6FB3`) stays unchanged pending Justin supplying the real value or an environment with broader web access.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-13 (orphaned GDPR module retirement)

- **`src/omen_gdpr.js` is retired rather than archived.** It was unmounted, duplicated the live privacy router, retained obsolete confirmation copy, constructed ad hoc clients outside `config.js`, and could log raw Vault secret identifiers. Probo evidence now targets the mounted `src/routes/userPrivacy.js`; historical records remain unchanged.

Flagged clearly in the sprint item and handoff so this isn't mistaken for "already applied to prod."

Verified WCAG contrast for both theme variants before landing (dark: ~10-12:1 against dark surfaces and near-black button text; light: 5.9:1 deepened teal against white, well above the 4.5:1 AA text threshold) — committed as `093d51a` on top of PR #116's merge, ahead of the Stripe removal work in this same session.


## Decisions Ratified 2026-07-19 (Native M0b/M1)

- **Focus is a first-class semantic token in M1.** Add `focus-ring` as a semantic token with a visible outline and platform-appropriate focus/selection behavior. It cannot depend on brass or any color alone.
- **Native typography is locked to Alegreya Sans, Alegreya, and DM Mono.** Alegreya Sans owns UI/headings/controls; Alegreya owns long-form reading copy; DM Mono owns scores, structured numeric values, and code-adjacent data. Do not revive Cinzel or Inter for native Omen UI.
- **Team-color tokens and team skins are out of the phone MVP.** Keep brass, verdigris, crimson, and semantic status roles stable. Team skins remain a future founder decision after core accessibility and parity.
- **M0b is inventory/rules; M1 is build briefs.** M0b documents tokens, component registry, states, accessibility, typography, platform differences, and theme boundaries. Per-component SwiftUI/Compose briefs and any component code start in M1.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-19 (Native build environment)

- **Do not require a Mac purchase for Omen native delivery.** Justin does not have a Mac capable of running Xcode and will not buy one. Native contracts and Figma work may continue on the current Windows workspace; M2 project scaffolding needs a separately selected, non-purchase iOS build/validation path and Android toolchain plan before code begins. Do not assume a paid hosted-Mac service or create cloud spend without explicit approval.
- **M2-E selected local Android plus zero-billed iOS simulator validation.** Android uses the Studio-managed SDK, ADB, emulator, and `Medium_Phone` Android 17/API 37.1 profile verified on this Windows machine. The iOS path is a future non-signing GitHub-hosted macOS simulator job, only when explicitly approved and only within included Actions capacity; if that capacity is unavailable, it blocks rather than incurs spend. No local Xcode, signing, TestFlight, store account, or real-iPhone validation is implied.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-19 (M2 native app-shell scaffold)

- **M2 is a compile-safe shell, not a provider or release implementation.** The new SwiftUI and Compose projects share the approved bundle/deep-link identifier, safe placeholder environment values, local demo entry, session state seam, and top-level navigation placeholders. They do not contain provider OAuth, secure-token persistence, backend calls, signing identities, App Store configuration, secrets, or production URLs.
- **Android is locally verified; iOS remains CI-gated.** The Android debug APK builds and launches on the local emulator. Because this Windows workspace has no Xcode-capable Mac, iOS project compilation must wait for a separately authorized, no-billed-usage macOS simulator CI job. This is a validation gap, not permission to add CI, signing, or TestFlight work.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-19 (M3 auth boundary)

- **The M3 sign-in surface is an explicitly temporary local placeholder.** It may never be described as authentication, account creation, provider connection, or release-ready behavior. It exists only to prove the local vertical-slice navigation and must remain visibly labeled until M3-A replaces it.
- **M3-A native authentication proof is mandatory before an external/native release.** The required scope is iOS Sign in with Apple plus email OTP, Android Credential Manager plus email OTP, Keychain/Keystore-backed session storage, named auth failure/recovery states, and safe real-device evidence. It requires separate founder authorization for public Supabase/auth configuration; provider connection, signing, and store release remain separate gates.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-19 (Apple Sign in provisioning)

- **Founder completed the Apple-provider configuration needed for M3-A without expanding the release scope.** Omen has a primary native App ID, a Supabase-facing Services ID, and an enabled Supabase Apple provider. Private key material and the generated OAuth secret remain outside Git and agent context.
- **Apple secret rotation is an operating requirement.** The generated OAuth secret expires after six months; the founder must rotate it before 2027-01-19 using the retained private key. Native iOS build, signing, TestFlight, store submission, and real-device proof remain separately gated.
- **The Apple capability catalogue is deferred V2 discovery, not an entitlement backlog.** Each proposed capability requires product value, privacy, App Review, operational, battery, and cost analysis before it is enabled.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-20 (M1-P P2 ListRow)

- **ListRow is the shared row primitive, not a feature-specific player row.** It carries title/subtitle text, optional leading/trailing slots, enabled/display-only behavior, and native interactive semantics. Feature-specific rows such as PlayerRow remain later compositions that consume this foundation instead of cloning row styling.
- **iOS ListRow uses a Button only when an action exists.** Display-only rows render as static content, while action rows preserve native button accessibility. This mirrors Android's optional clickable ListItem behavior and keeps non-interactive list content from advertising a false action.
- **SwiftUI stored view slots are plain stored values.** The `@ViewBuilder` annotations live on initializer closures only; storing built `Leading` and `Trailing` values avoids a compile-risk pattern and was validated by unsigned simulator CI.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-21 (M1-P P4 gallery + enforcement)

- **P4 enforcement is a source-scanning test on both platforms, not a lint plugin.** Android `:core:designsystem` `PrimitiveEnforcementTest` (JUnit) and iOS `OmenIOSTests/PrimitiveEnforcementTests` (XCTest) walk feature/app-shell sources and fail on raw Material 3 primitives, raw SwiftUI primitives, or raw color literals. `detekt` and `SwiftLint` are not adopted in this PR — the scanners carry the rule until a broader lint pass lands and are deletable at that point.
- **`OmenAndroidApp.kt` is allowlisted with a written retirement plan, not refactored in this PR.** The M2 demo shell holds raw `androidx.compose.material3` primitives and `Color(0xNN...)` literals; rebuilding the shell from approved primitives is M4 shell work, not P4 scope. The allowlist entry retires when the first M4 feature screen ships and the shell is composed from `Omen*` primitives.
- **iOS enforcement allowlist starts empty.** The iOS `App/` subtree was already `Omen*`-only when P4 landed. Any future exemption must document a written reason + retirement plan in `PrimitiveEnforcementTests.allowlistedRelativePaths`, same standard as the Android side.
- **Debug-only gallery on both platforms.** The SwiftUI gallery is wrapped in `#if DEBUG`; the Android gallery activity lives in the `debug` source-set. Release binaries carry no gallery code.
- **Skill improvement candidate:** the negative-check-then-restore verification pattern (temporarily disable the allowlist, confirm the scanner fails, restore) is worth adding to `slops-code-review` for any future scanner-style test. Not blocking this PR.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-23 (M0-BE-2 native connection retries)

- **Native Sleeper connect uses a bounded Redis replay record rather than a schema change.** The existing `platform_connections` upsert prevents duplicate rows but cannot return a safe first-response replay after response loss. The already-configured Redis dependency records an authenticated user/request ID outcome for 10 minutes, avoiding new provider credentials, migrations, or production-data action. Replay-store unavailability fails closed before the connection write; the timeout is deliberately not described as indefinite idempotency.
- **Yahoo and ESPN stay out of the retry implementation.** Yahoo's OAuth transaction/return has different authorization-artifact and web-compatibility obligations, so it remains M0-BE-3 behind current primary-source research. ESPN has no approved native-store path and is unchanged.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-26 (B2-D-S4 off-season handling)

- **Sleeper waiver ships live but routes to the off-season surface until the season opens.** Founder decision 2026-07-26, resolving S4 option (a) in `Blueprints/specs/b2d-live-waiver-pool-sleeper-espn-v1.md`. Preseason projections are labeled as estimates and never carry in-season confidence. The alternative — staying dark until week 1 — was rejected.
- **`pre_draft` is a third state, distinct from off-season.** Surfaced by the S3 attempt: a league that has not drafted has no rosters, so every eligible player is correctly "available". A waiver recommendation there is not merely out of season, it is meaningless, and a naive implementation would present 3,293 players as waiver opportunities. `GET /v1/league/{id}` exposes `status`; S2 must branch on in-season, off-season, and pre-draft rather than a season/off-season binary.
- **A pre-draft league cannot prove roster subtraction.** The 2026-07-26 S3 run against a real 12-team league succeeded end to end (719 ms, 3,293 pool, 533 projected, ranking correct) but every roster was empty placeholders, so nothing was subtracted. Returning the full pool is the correct answer for an undrafted league, which is exactly why the run cannot distinguish a working subtraction from a broken one. S3 stays open until a drafted league is available.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-27 (M4 Help + Support contract)

- **Contextual Help and Help + Support are separate native jobs.** A nearby Tooltip/Help affordance explains a current concept or state and returns to the exact prior work; Account's Support & Help Improve Omen is the durable home for Help Center guidance, voluntary feedback, feature ideas, and problem reporting. The responsive web HelpButton is content inventory only, not the native layout authority.
- **Native support starts as an approval-gated composition, not a backend integration.** The Design House proposal uses existing Tooltip/Help, Modal/Sheet, ListRow, Button, IconButton, and State Surface contracts and existing semantic tokens. It creates no support inbox, ticket API, telemetry, provider flow, credential handling, or silent submission queue.
- **Feedback is intentionally privacy-minimal.** Selected league/roster data defaults off, and credentials, cookies, OAuth tokens, raw provider errors, and hidden session data must never attach automatically. Offline/no-account/unavailable states remain honest and do not claim delivery.
- **Founder approval recorded.** Justin approved the Help + Support Design House proposal and its iOS/Android placement nodes (`61:2`, `63:2`, `63:26`) on 2026-07-27. This clears the visual-contract gate only; the separately scoped native implementation still must obey the exact file, privacy, provider, and support-backend boundaries in `m4-help-support-v1.md`.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-27 (M4 Help + Support implementation)

- **Support feedback stays locally unavailable until a separate backend contract exists.** The iOS and Android actions disclose that nothing is sent or saved, rather than creating a mock submission, queue, telemetry event, or support endpoint.
- **Account is the durable entry point on both platforms.** iOS pushes Help + Support in the existing Account navigation stack; Android layers the approved Help + Support sheet over Account so system Back returns to Account before dismissing it.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-27 (D2 closeout)

- **The D2 $0 cloud boundary is implemented work, not deployment proof.** PRs #225 and #226 merged the local-default, cloud-disabled control and its evidence records. No production environment change, cloud credential, paid service, or live-provider validation is implied by that completion.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-28 (security hardening landing)

- **OAuth artifacts are operationally sensitive metadata.** Production access logs retain only route-level operational fields, while query strings, referrers, and user agents are omitted; Sentry additionally scrubs OAuth `code` and `state` query parameters.
- **Waitlist RLS source is not a database action.** The tracked policy source records server-only intent, but production application remains separately approved after a read-only client-usage check. Merging this code must never be represented as a Supabase mutation or live security proof.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-28 (M0-BE-3 evidence reconciliation)

- **Yahoo mobile return is implemented source, not provider/device proof.** PR #191's server-bound native return is verified locally: the app receives only a fixed `connected` or `cancelled` status after server-side state validation. Yahoo console configuration and a real-device browser-to-app cycle remain human gates and cannot be inferred from source tests.
- **Living native contracts must state the implemented boundary.** Current M0c/onboarding/contract-bus language now points to the merged route; historical 2026-07-19 planning records remain historical rather than being rewritten.
## Decisions Added 2026-07-26 (B2-D2 guarded Yahoo waiver fallback)

- **Yahoo waiver advice is availability-based until a projection-capable source is proven.** The existing Yahoo available-player path is ordered by Yahoo average rank but does not provide a weekly projection. Canonical Omen may therefore suggest only a live same-position replacement for an OUT/IR starter, with a null point delta and explicit unavailable-projection signal; it must not imply a projection-backed ranking.
- **Yahoo waiver retrieval fails closed.** If the selected-context availability request fails or lacks an eligible replacement, canonical Omen returns no advice rather than the legacy optimizer's mock waiver fixture. Real-account capability proof remains blocked on Yahoo Fantasy API reapproval.
- **Do not query a waiver pool without an injury-replacement need.** If the selected Yahoo roster has no OUT/IR-like starter, return the honest live empty envelope before available-player retrieval. This minimizes provider access and keeps the fallback scoped to the promised availability-based recovery case.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-29 (Claude work recovery)

- **A recovery merge must preserve newer provider behavior.** The older deterministic-selector branch would have removed the later Yahoo availability-only fallback. The recovered selector ranks only scoreable candidates and then retains that separate null-point-delta fallback when there is no scoreable decision.
- **Provider-proof scripts report uncertainty rather than manufacturing success.** The public Sleeper roster-subtraction script uses no credential and returns `UNDECIDABLE` for an undrafted/empty-roster league. A future drafted-league run is required before claiming real roster subtraction.
- **Stale PRs are closed with replacement links after recovery.** Original #220/#222/#223/#224 are closed as superseded by current-main recoveries #240/#241/#242/#243; this records ledger continuity without calling historical branch tips current.
## Decisions Added 2026-07-26 (Sleeper waiver foundation — B2-D-S0/S1)

- **Fixtures standing in for an external API response must be captured from a real payload, not authored by hand.** `test/sleeperAdapter.test.js` fixtured Sleeper projections as an object map with flat `pts_ppr`. The live endpoint has never returned that shape — it returns an array with points under `stats`. The suite passed while production resolved every projection to null, for the life of the adapter. A green test over an invented external fixture is not evidence. Any such fixture should cite the date and request it was captured from.
- **Sleeper projections are keyed by `player_id`, never by array position.** A string id used as an array index resolves by position, which for low-numbered veteran ids returns a *different player's* record. This was masked by a second defect (points nested under `stats`), so fixing only the nesting would have converted silent-null into silently-wrong points attributed to the wrong player. Normalization happens once in `normalizeProjections`, and cached values are normalized on read so pre-fix cache entries degrade to a miss rather than a wrong-player match.
- **A derived availability pool must fail toward "owned", never toward "available".** Sleeper has no free-agent endpoint, so the waiver pool is computed as all eligible players minus all rostered players. A roster row without a usable array is treated as unknown-but-owned. Erring toward a smaller pool loses an opportunity; erring the other way offers a player the user cannot actually add, presented as live advice. All four roster arrays (`players`, `starters`, `reserve`, `taxi`) are unioned.
- **An absent projection is `null`, never `0`.** Zero reads as a real projection of zero rather than missing data, and `Number(x) || 0` downstream would silently equate the two. Unprojected players sort last rather than being dropped, so they remain visible as candidates without implying a forecast.
- **Fantasy eligibility, not primary position, decides whether a player belongs in the pool.** Sleeper lists fullbacks as `position: "FB"` with `fantasy_positions: ["RB"]`; they are rosterable at RB. A primary-position filter withheld 74 active players, 4 of them projected. The same rule correctly drops players listed at a skill position with no fantasy eligibility (a TE marked `["OL"]`, a K marked `["DL"]`).
- **Live-scale verification against a synthetic roster set is not capability proof.** The S1 checks used a real player universe and real projections but an empty roster set, which proves eligibility, the projection join, and ranking — not the roster subtraction, which is the stated primary risk. Capability proof requires a real league and remains S3.

## Decisions Added 2026-07-29 (Sleeper S3 live proof)

- **Sleeper roster subtraction is provider-proven for the supplied drafted league.** The credential-free public probe of league `1387633793615036416` observed 120 held player IDs and zero leaks in the 3,174-player waiver pool. It also observed 415 projected players and correct null-last ordering. This proves the adapter’s public-pool subtraction behavior for that real league; it does not authorize a transaction, deployment, or a broader claim about another provider.
## 2026-08-02 — Sleeper trade capability is selected-context, privacy-sanitized, and lineup-first

- `POST /api/omen/mvp-move` may now emit a `trade_suggestion` only for a selected Sleeper connection when no scoreable Start/Sit or waiver move exists. It fetches the same public league's normalized roster surface, identifies the selected roster by its opaque roster ID, and never emits a Sleeper manager display name, username, user ID, or avatar.
- The candidate evaluator is intentionally one-for-one for v1. It computes each side's optimal weekly lineup from the league's slots, excludes IR/taxi from lineup totals while retaining them as tradeable assets, requires strictly positive weekly improvement for both teams, and applies `compareTrade()` through an explicit VORP-loss-per-weekly-point threshold. It makes no offer-acceptance, package, pick, or future-week guarantee.
- Credential-free public proof against a drafted Sleeper league produced only aggregate evidence: 8 rosters, 120 rostered players, 119 projection-joined player rows, and a sanitized output shape. This is provider-read proof, not a transaction, production, or deployment claim.

## 2026-08-02 — ESPN waiver work is decomposed before implementation

- The remaining B2-D ESPN waiver gap is three outcomes, not one opaque task: normalize the provider pool with per-entry ownership checks, wire truthful selected-context candidates into canonical Omen, then collect drafted-league proof. `onTeamId === 0` is mandatory regardless of the server filter result; drafted-league evidence remains a final provider-proof gate and never requires cookie access.

## 2026-08-02 — ESPN E1 normalizes provider data before it can affect a recommendation

- `fetchEspnWaiverPool()` is an adapter-only boundary. It requests `kona_player_info` with the provider filter in `x-fantasy-filter`, paginates until a short page, and applies `onTeamId === 0` again while normalizing each response entry. The server filter is an optimization, not an ownership claim.
- Only `statSourceId === 1` from the requested scoring period supplies `projected_points`; actual statistics are never substituted. The function has no canonical Omen, route, database, cache, or UI wiring, so E2 remains the separate selected-context integration task.

## 2026-08-02 — ESPN E2 reuses one selected-context credential read for roster and waiver evaluation

- Canonical Omen validates the selected opaque context before retrieving the selected ESPN connection's existing Vault-backed session. That in-memory session is reused for the roster and waiver-pool reads; unselected ESPN connections are not decrypted or queried.
- ESPN waiver evaluation opens only for an unavailable starter, considers only projected same-position candidates, and ranks them by weekly projection rather than ESPN's popularity order. Provider failure produces a live empty response with an unavailable waiver signal; a successful empty pool remains honestly live-empty. No mock candidate is substituted.

## 2026-08-02 — ESPN drafted-league roster subtraction is provider-proven

- A founder-authorized read-only run used the one complete ESPN connection updated immediately after the Chrome-extension handoff. The session and league context remained in memory only for the two ESPN reads; recorded evidence contains counts and timings only.
- The drafted league contained 10 populated teams and 160 distinct rostered players. ESPN returned a 500-entry filtered pool with 0 rostered-player overlaps, 0 non-zero `onTeamId` values, and 0 ownership-signal disagreements. Observation 12 therefore closes cleanly, while E1's independent `onTeamId === 0` guard remains mandatory defense in depth.
- This is provider capability proof, not evidence that the stacked E1/E2 commits are pushed, merged, deployed, or working through the production canonical route.

## 2026-08-02 — B2-D evidence reconciliation

- The preceding E1/E2 entries accurately described their state at the time they were written, but are now stale: adapter PR #265 (`171508f`) and canonical-wiring PR #266 (`623068a`) are ancestors of `main`.
- B2-D's documented completion is merged-code, fixture, and sanitized provider-proof evidence. It does not assert a deployment, real-account canonical-route run, or a public all-platform-live claim.

- Omen is available only to people age 13 or older. A person initiating sign-in confirms the 13+ boundary, agrees to the `2026-08-02` Terms, and acknowledges the `2026-08-02` Privacy Notice; the authenticated server records those exact versions without trusting a client-supplied user ID.

## 2026-08-02 — LEGAL-V1 is live through the normal KVM1 release path

- PR #269 passed every required PR check and squash-merged as `64305c1b322ed639f282c41c220cbe47836f4aff`.
- Deploy run `30769488793` passed clean-runner quality, GHCR API/cron image publication, KVM1 pull/restart, health smoke, deployed-asset verification, and public-route smoke.
- Independent read-only canary returned 200 with HSTS across three rounds for apex and `www` health/readiness and the changed public legal routes; HTTP redirected to HTTPS, observed p95 was 350 ms, and the live bundle contained the exact final-v1 legal markers.
- No production-data write was used as proof. Live Resend delivery, legal-acceptance persistence, waitlist removal, and account/Auth deletion remain unexercised in production. Rollback is a revert of `64305c1b` through the normal main workflow to rebuild the prior `8c2368d` source.
- **2026-08-13 — Native auth callbacks and email fallback stay native.** Supabase owns the top-level OAuth `state`; Omen embeds its CSRF state in the allow-listed native `redirect_to` and validates it before PKCE exchange. Android uses a single-task callback activity; iOS handles the URL captured by `ASWebAuthenticationSession` directly, retaining `onOpenURL` as a cold-launch fallback. M3A email remains a six-digit OTP: hosted templates emit `{{ .Token }}`, server OTP length is six, and clients normalize only surrounding whitespace. Production SMTP uses an external Resend sending-only key scoped to `slopssaloon.com`; no credential belongs in repository or client configuration. Founder identities are not destructive QA fixtures, so deletion proof requires a disposable account or explicit approval at that step.

## 2026-08-14 — Yahoo connections paused behind one flag; Draft Assistant may be named as 2027

- **Yahoo is paused at the entry point, not cancelled.** Every Yahoo code path — OAuth round-trip, league binding (#293), the Connect buttons (#292), the multi-provider waiver gate, and the availability-only waiver fallback — is merged and VERIFIED, and the suite passes 535/535. The only missing piece is Yahoo's own Fantasy Sports API entitlement for app `ZcZJXm8V`, re-applied for 2026-08-13. That is a review queue outside this codebase, so the work is **dormant, not abandoned**.
- **Why pause at all, given the code works.** Since #292 fixed the Connect buttons, the Yahoo OAuth handshake *succeeds*. A user could connect Yahoo, get a live `platform_connections` row reading `connected`, and then have every Fantasy call return 403 — a connection that looks healthy and serves nothing. That is `connected ≠ usable` (facts-of-record #12) pointed at users instead of at us, and it is also the kind of visibly broken flow that draws an App Store 2.1 rejection. Offering the button was worse than either "post-beta" or "not yet".
- **Chosen posture: post-beta, entry point disabled — not merely relabelled.** Yahoo stays visible and labelled "On hold" so Yahoo users can see it is coming; what is removed is the ability to *start* a connection. Existing Yahoo rows remain visible and **disconnectable**, so anyone who connected before the pause is not stranded with a row they cannot act on.
- **The pause is exactly one flag, by design.** `YAHOO_ENABLED` (`config.yahoo.enabled`, default **false**) gates `GET`/`POST /api/yahoo/auth` with a 503. `GET /api/yahoo/callback` is deliberately **not** gated, so an in-flight consent round-trip cannot strand. `GET /api/yahoo/access-probe` is deliberately **not** gated, because it is the re-check mechanism. `/api/platforms` and `/api/platforms/status` now carry `yahoo.available` and `yahoo.unavailableReason` so the UI has one source of truth. The frontend additionally hard-codes `YAHOO_CONNECTIONS_ENABLED` in `frontend/src/lib/yahooAuth.js` as a last line of defence for screens that never loaded platform status.
- **To re-enable when Yahoo approves:** set `YAHOO_ENABLED=true` on **both** `omen_api` and `omen_cron`, redeploy, and flip `YAHOO_CONNECTIONS_ENABLED` to `true`. No other change. `test/platforms.test.js` locks this in with a test that flipping the flag reports Yahoo available again.
- **Do not delete Yahoo code, tests, or fixtures as "dead".** They are the thing that makes re-enabling a flag flip rather than a rebuild.
- **Draft Assistant may now be named, with a fixed date and only outside store metadata.** The 2026-08-05 cut and 2026-08-11 sideline stand: it is not a 1.0 feature and its implementation stays preserved. What changes is that a **single factual forward-looking mention** is permitted — the approved wording is **"2027 fantasy draft"** (founder: "next fantasy year's draft"). Never "coming soon", never a month, never a phrasing that implies it is in this version.
- **Store metadata stays excluded from that permission.** Apple and Google both expect listing copy to describe what the app currently does; advertising an unreleased feature in the listing invites a review rejection and is a different risk from saying it on our own site. Marketing site copy and a clearly-labelled in-app "not in this version" note are permitted; store metadata, onboarding flows implying availability, navigation entries, legal copy, and the advertised tool list in `/api/dashboard/summary` remain prohibited.

## 2026-08-15 — Contextual Help ports the web's *content*, never its *claims*

- **The `Tooltip/Help` primitive was specified but never built.** `m4-help-support-v1` §2 says the contextual affordance uses "the **existing** Tooltip/Help primitive". It is a registry §3.1 row, but it was omitted from the M1-P P2 foundation build list, and neither app contained the string `tooltip`. M6 therefore built the primitive before it could wire anything. Treated as covered by the already-approved Help + Support Figma nodes (`61:2`/`63:2`/`63:26`), whose §3 anatomy lists `Tooltip/Help` as a member — a documentary reading, not a Figma-verified one, since the Figma connector was unauthenticated that session.
- **Help attaches to shipped destinations only — four, not six.** Trade and League still render "landing next" state surfaces. Explaining a screen that does not exist is worse than offering no help, so both are excluded and a test pins the omission with instructions to delete itself when those screens ship.
- **The web `PAGE_HELP` map is a content inventory, not a source of truth about the product.** Two of its entries are actively false on native: it advertises **Draft Assistant** (cut from 1.0) and tells people to **connect ESPN** (impossible in-app; ESPN is a website-only connection here, and Yahoo is paused). Copy was mined and rewritten, never ported.
- **Those corrections are tests, not review notes.** Both platforms assert: no "draft assistant" anywhere; every claim mentioning ESPN must also route to the website; no mention of cookies; the only permitted "password" sentence is the promise Omen never asks for one. The Yahoo and ESPN sentences are additionally pinned character-for-character against `ConnectProvider.availability`, so provider copy and the connect screen cannot silently diverge. Review catches a bad copy edit once; a test catches it every time.
- **Help copy is a place where a platform split is a defect, not a platform difference.** The iOS and Android tables are word-identical, and the Android test pins the provider sentences against the iOS wording. The one place they were about to diverge — "sign in with Face ID" — was rewritten to "sign in without waiting on an email code", which is true on a Touch ID device and on Android too.
- **A green suite is not a correct render.** Every content rule passed on the first Android run. At font scale 1.8 the sheet's last line still ran under the gesture navigation bar — invisible to all of them, obvious in a screenshot. Fixed with `navigationBarsPadding()`. For any text surface, capture it at a large font scale before calling it done.

## 2026-08-15 — iOS accessibility evidence is an audit, not VoiceOver, and the difference is recorded

- **The iOS Simulator cannot run VoiceOver at all.** `com.apple.VoiceOverTouch` is registered with launchd as a `LimitLoadToSessionType = Background` job and never acquires a PID; setting `VoiceOverTouchEnabled` and resprining does not change it. Any past or future claim of "VoiceOver verified" that rests on a simulator run is false by construction.
- **Substitute chosen: `XCUIApplication.performAccessibilityAudit()`**, which walks the same accessibility tree VoiceOver consumes and checks element descriptions, contrast, hit regions, clipped text, and traits. This required creating the repo's first UI-test target, `OmenIOSUITests`, registered in the shared `OmenIOS` scheme — so `xcodebuild test -scheme OmenIOS` now runs UI tests too and takes roughly 65s longer. **An audit proves an announcement exists and is well-formed; it cannot judge whether it is useful.** A real-device VoiceOver pass is still owed.
- **Android needed no substitute.** TalkBack was installed, enabled, confirmed *bound* with touch exploration on, and driven against the app: the affordance announced its label, its node measured 48dp, TalkBack's own double-tap opened the sheet, and System Back dismissed it back to the originating screen. TalkBack does not log utterances at any public tag, so the artifact is the accessibility tree plus proof the service was bound and holding speech audio focus — not a transcript.
- **The audit immediately earned its cost by catching an AA contrast defect I had shipped.** `text-secondary` on `surface-2` measures **4.43:1** in light mode, under the 4.5:1 floor. Registry §3.1's Tooltip/Help row specifies `surface-2` + **`text-primary`**; I had not followed it. Fixed on both platforms. **The token pair remains available to every other component**, so this is now a design-system risk in `known_issues.md`, not a closed item.
- **Two findings were classified as pre-existing rather than fixed, deliberately.** The Command Center screen fails `contrast` outright, and `OmenTypography` is invisible to the audit's Dynamic Type check app-wide because it vends `Font(UIFontMetrics.scaledFont(for:))` instead of a text-style-relative font. Both are outside M6's approved file list. The first is pinned under `XCTExpectFailure` so it fails loudly when someone fixes it; the second was checked *behaviorally* — rendering the same surface at `UICTContentSizeCategoryM` and `AccessibilityXXXL` shows correct scaling and reflow — and so is recorded as a mechanism finding, with `.dynamicType` excluded from the audit and the reasoning kept at the exclusion site. **Neither was silently suppressed.**
- **An audit verdict is a hypothesis until the behavior is checked.** "Dynamic Type font sizes are unsupported" reads like a functional failure and is not one. The same discipline that rejects a green build as proof of a working feature applies to a red audit as proof of a broken one.

## 2026-08-15 — ESPN is a web connection Omen actively wants, not an unavailable provider

- **Correction to the same day's M6 entry.** During M6 verification I flagged the Command Center matchup hero's "connect Sleeper or ESPN" as a false capability claim. **It is not.** ESPN connects — once, on the Omen website — and the league then appears in the app. Omen wants those connections. What native lacks is only the in-app credential handoff, and the in-app path already handles that honestly: choosing ESPN in Connect reaches `ConnectProvider.espn` → `.useWeb`, which routes the person to the website rather than dead-ending.
- **Where the error came from.** `M6-ContextualHelp`'s own sprint text says "on native, ESPN cannot be connected at all", and I carried that phrasing into a judgement instead of checking it against `ConnectProvider.availability`, which says `.useWeb` — a *route*, not an absence. A queue item's framing is a claim to verify, not a premise to reason from; this is the second time that pattern has cost something in this repo.
- **The distinction that matters for all future copy.** Yahoo is `.onHold`: it cannot be connected anywhere right now, so copy states a pause. ESPN is `.useWeb`: it can be connected today by a path we want people to take, so copy must name that path. **Collapsing the two into "unavailable" is the defect** — it would strand every ESPN user rather than direct them.
- **Guarded against over-correction in code.** The "any ESPN sentence must name the website" test would also pass if someone deleted ESPN from the help copy entirely. Both platforms now additionally assert that the Connect topic **still offers ESPN**, so satisfying the rule by removing the mention fails the suite.

## 2026-08-21 — `O8` + `O9`: error tracking wired end to end, and both layers were found broken in the shape of good news

- **The ratified Option D split is now real, not just decided.** Backend → **GlitchTip** on Command Center; mobile/browser → **Sentry SaaS**. `O8` pointed production's backend `SENTRY_DSN` at GlitchTip, which is what Option D specifies and what the pre-existing sentry.io backend DSN contradicted — Option D deliberately keeps bursty backend volume off the metered SaaS path, and "no Express/backend Sentry project was created" was the stated point of the split. The malformed value found in production referenced a backend Sentry org anyway; correcting it aligned production with the ratified decision rather than merely fixing a typo.
- **`O8`'s own Scope was written on a false premise, and following it literally would have made things worse.** It claimed nothing in `src/` sent anything and therefore instructed hand-rolling an HTTP integration to avoid the dependency gate. `@sentry/node` had been wired since Phase 1.2 (`cc14e79`), so the gate did not apply and the instruction would have added a second, weaker reporting path beside a working one. **Generalise the standing "`main` is the proof" rule explicitly to absence claims** — it is applied reflexively to "X is done" and almost never to "X does not exist", yet the absence claim is the easier one to get wrong, because nothing contradicts it until someone looks.
- **Production had never reported a single error, to any tool** — see the correction above and #354. The guard shipped with `O8`: the DSN is validated rather than truthiness-checked, the validator matches the SDK's own key grammar (a validator looser than what it validates is a second bug wearing the guard's clothes — found when it passed GlitchTip's dashed-UUID key that `@sentry/node` rejects), and `GET /api/ready` surfaces `checks.error_tracking` with host and project id but never the key.
- **`O9` then found the alerting layer could not deliver the alert it exists for.** Adding GlitchTip as a fourth signal source produced the Discord dispatcher's first genuinely multi-line signature, which failed with `400` — the payload was string-interpolated into JSON, and raw newlines are invalid inside a JSON string. **Every alert this fleet had ever proven carried exactly one failing signal**, so a simultaneous two-signal failure would have sent nothing. A second defect kept that silent: state was persisted before delivery was confirmed, so the failed send recorded its signature and the next run saw no change. Two bugs, each hiding the other.
- **The through-line, and the reason both survived so long: monitoring and alerting fail in the shape of good news.** Quiet is the success signal and the failure signal at once. Neither defect was visible to any "is it configured?" check; both surfaced only by exercising the real path with real content. **Codify two things.** First, for observability systems, acceptance requires a positive liveness proof, never a configuration check. Second, **a test that exercises one item does not test a list** — Layer 5's noise-control semantics were genuinely proven at build time, but the delivery mechanism was only ever tested on its easiest possible input, and the two were proven in the same breath.


## 2026-08-22 — `S3` + `S4` + `O4`: the rate limit is keyed on the credential, the scrubber is a boundary, and the load number is chosen against the limit

PR [#355](https://github.com/justinduverge-design/omen/pull/355) — open, unmerged, founder review pending. Nothing deployed.

### The per-user rate limit is keyed on the credential, not on `sub` — and the difference is written into the contract

`S3` requires a per-user limit on three routes. The limiters run **before** authentication: they are mounted ahead of the routers, and `POST /api/omen/mvp-move` authenticates inside its own handler. So there is no verified user id at the moment the decision has to be made.

Decoding the JWT and keying on its `sub` claim is the obvious move and was rejected. An unverified `sub` is attacker-supplied: anyone can mint a token carrying a victim's `sub`, spend the victim's budget, and lock them out of the most important route in the product. That converts a defensive control into an availability attack on a named user.

**Decision: key the user bucket on a SHA-256 digest of the presented bearer token.** An attacker cannot enter another bucket without already holding that credential, at which point they are that user for every other purpose too.

**The cost is real and is documented rather than hidden.** This is *per-credential*, not *per-account*: two devices get two buckets, and a Supabase token refresh (~hourly) mints a fresh one, so a determined authenticated client can reset their own budget by refreshing. The per-IP limit is the backstop that keeps that bounded — which is the reason both limits are enforced rather than either alone. Stated in `Blueprints/api-routes.md` § Rate Limits and in the module header, because the next person to read "per-user limit" will otherwise assume it means per-account.

**Corollary recorded in the same place:** the limiters stack rather than replace. `O4`'s saturation run showed that for a client using the whole app, the app-wide 100/min/IP limiter binds before `GET /api/dashboard/summary`'s own 60/min does. A route's documented budget is a ceiling, not a promise of that much throughput.

### Credential containment is a boundary property now, not a call-site convention

Before `S4`, "no credential in the logs" held because ~40 call sites each remembered to pass `err.message` rather than the error, and `sanitizedError(e)` rather than `e`. The convention was being followed. It is also exactly the kind of guarantee that holds until the next field is added, and whose failure mode is silence.

**Decision: every winston line leaves through the shared scrubber, and the terminal error handler moved into `src/middleware/errorEnvelope.js` so the message it echoes back is scrubbed too.** Call sites still pass only what they need; this is the backstop for when one of them stops. The handler moved out of `server.js` for a second reason worth stating: a test that re-declares the envelope proves the test's copy is safe, which is not the claim `S4` has to make.

**Two live gaps were found by this, and neither would have been found by reading the code.**

`authorization` was absent from the sensitive-key pattern. An axios failure carries `error.config.headers` — every other key on that object was covered and this one was not. And once added, `authorization: Bearer ya29.x` *still* leaked, because the key/value rule stops a value at the first space: it redacted the word "Bearer" and published the token behind it. **The rule reported success on the exact string it was failing to protect.** `Bearer` and `Basic` now have their own rule, applied first, and deliberately not extended to `OAuth` — that challenge is scheme plus `key=value` params, already covered, and blanket-redacting it would destroy the `oauth_problem` diagnostic that made the Yahoo 403 tractable.

This is the third consecutive session in which the shared scrubber was found to have a hole. `O8` found `\b` before `token` skipping `access_token=`; `O8` found JSON-quoted secrets passing through; `S4` found `authorization` and the space-terminated value. **The pattern is not that the scrubber is badly written — it is that a redaction rule only covers the shapes someone actually fed it.** Every one of these was found by provoking a real failure and searching the emitted bytes, and none by review.

**A boundary is recorded rather than quietly left:** a credential logged as a bare value with no key beside it is invisible to the text scrubber. No code path does that, and facts-of-record #6 keeps ESPN cookie values out of every emission site by construction. It is written into the test so the next reader knows what the backstop does and does not cover, instead of inheriting an assumption that it covers everything.

### The load-test concurrency is derived from the rate limits and from the repo

`O4`'s numbers only mean something relative to `S3`, which landed the day before. Two failure modes were available and both produce a confident, useless figure: measure before the limits and you have characterised a system that no longer exists; measure after them without accounting for them and past ~20 requests/minute you are timing `express-rate-limit`.

**Decision: bound requests-per-client by the tightest per-credential budget, and take concurrency off the repo rather than out of the air.** 8 requests per client sits under all three budgets (`mvp-move`'s 10/min is the binding one). 20 concurrent is the top of the K2 beta cohort — the whole cohort arriving in the same instant. 200 concurrent is R6's internal-track ceiling, 100 iOS plus 100 Android with no Beta App Review, so the 10× the item asks for lands on a real boundary.

**Second decision: give every simulated client its own source IP and credential.** A generator on one machine is one IP and one credential, which is not what production load looks like — N users arrive from N networks and each spends their own budget. Collapsing the run into a single bucket would have measured the limiter and reported it as latency. Then run the opposite deliberately, one identity, as separate evidence *about the limiter* — which is how the general-limiter interaction above was found.

**Third: a local load test that 401s is not a load test.** `scripts/local-load-stack.js` boots the real server against a loopback Supabase stub so `GET /api/dashboard/summary` measures the real hot path. Without it, `requireAuth` reaches the configured Supabase host on every request and the numbers are DNS and TLS timings against a third party. The stub 404s loudly on any unstubbed path, per `scripts/README.md`'s rule against false all-clears.

**And the scope caveat is recorded next to the numbers, not below them.** No provider fan-out, no real LLM call, `mvp-move` in explicit mock mode, loopback on one host. `O4`'s own Scope says Week 1 Sunday morning is the real load test. The honest reading of p95 ≤ 107 ms at 0 % error is *nothing in Omen's own code is a bottleneck at beta scale* — not *Omen is fast*.

## 2026-08-23 — Google Play Data Safety and its prerequisite declarations were submitted

- **Founder-submitted result:** Play Console lists Data Safety as an actioned declaration dated 2026-08-23. Its summary records three collected/shared data categories, no third-party sharing, encryption in transit, and `https://slopssaloon.com/delete-account` as both the account-deletion URL and deletion-support URL.
- **Prerequisites completed in the same founder session:** the live Privacy Notice URL is `https://slopssaloon.com/privacy`; App access states that some functionality is restricted and directs reviewers to the pre-authentication `Try Demo` path without credentials; Target audience selects 13–15, 16–17, and 18+, with no group under 13 and no child-directed claim.
- **Scope:** this clears only Google Play's half of `R4`. Apple privacy nutrition labels are still required before `R4` closes, and no internal-test rollout or public release was authorized by submitting these declarations.

## 2026-08-23 — Both stores' privacy and age declarations are complete

Email, user content, user ID, product interaction, and other data are linked to identity; diagnostics are not. No type is used for tracking. `https://slopssaloon.com/privacy` is the Privacy Policy URL and `https://slopssaloon.com/delete-account` is the User Privacy Choices URL.
- **Apple age-rating result:** every questionnaire page was read back before save. The bounded 30-day public trade-share path is declared as user-generated content; unrestricted web access, social media, messaging/chat, advertising, mature content, medical/wellness guidance, violence, real or simulated gambling, contests, and loot boxes are No/None. The founder chose the 13+ higher-rating override because Omen's Terms require users to be at least 13; App Store Connect reports 13+ in 172 countries/regions and Apple's regional equivalents elsewhere.
- **Cross-store result and reasoning:** Google Play's Data Safety and IARC declarations were already submitted in the same founder session. The two stores now describe the same product boundaries: no ads, no tracking, no wagering, no purchases, bounded UGC only, and a 13+ product policy. `R4` and `R5` therefore close. Under the founder's 2026-08-22 conditional approval, `R6` private internal testing is now unblocked; this does not authorize an external test or public store release.
